# The renderer

_Generated by docgen. Do not edit._

**Headers:** `engine/renderer/bounds.h`, `engine/renderer/camera.h`, `engine/renderer/device_resources.h`, `engine/renderer/frame_data.h`, `engine/renderer/frustum.h`, `engine/renderer/gbuffer.h`, `engine/renderer/glyph_atlas_texture.h`, `engine/renderer/lighting.h`, `engine/renderer/material.h`, `engine/renderer/mesh.h`, `engine/renderer/render_graph.h`, `engine/renderer/render_scene.h`, `engine/renderer/render_settings.h`, `engine/renderer/renderer.h`, `engine/renderer/renderer_info.h`, `engine/renderer/renderer_log.h`, `engine/renderer/scene_frame.h`, `engine/renderer/shader_permutation.h`, `engine/renderer/shader_reflection.h`, `engine/renderer/shared_settings.h`, `engine/renderer/text_renderer.h`, `engine/renderer/texture.h`, `engine/renderer/transient_pool.h`, `engine/renderer/ui_renderer.h`, `engine/renderer/ui_surface_target.h`, `engine/renderer/viewport_target.h`

Bounding volumes used for culling. The types and functions moved to `math/geometry.h` (Phase 18 Day 238), shared with the spatial module; the renderer keeps its names for them here, so no caller changes.

Cameras and the matrices they produce, in the conventions every renderer pass shares.

* World and view space are right-handed with Y up; a camera looks down -Z in view space. * [`renderer::PerspectiveVulkan`](#renderer-perspectivevulkan-f32-f32-f32-f32) maps view space to Vulkan clip space: X right, Y *down* (the matrix flips Y) and depth 0..1 (near -> 0, far -> 1, no reverse-Z). Viewports take a positive height; the flip lives in the projection. * Meshes are counter-clockwise seen from outside. With the projection above that is a front face for `FrontFace::CounterClockwise`, and `CullMode::Back` removes faces that point away. * Matrices are column-major ([`Mat4`](foundation-math.md#mat4)), which is std140's layout for a `mat4`, so one can be copied into a uniform buffer as it is.

The registry of everything in the renderer that owns device objects and can rebuild them after the device is replaced.

The data every frame feeds to the shaders: one `FrameData` uniform and one storage buffer of per-object data, both rewritten each frame. A draw picks its object by instance index, so no bind group is created per draw.

The view frustum and the tests that decide whether an object can be seen. The types moved to `math/geometry.h` (Phase 18 Day 238), shared with the spatial module; the renderer keeps its names for them here, so no caller changes. The frustum is built from a matrix of [`renderer::PerspectiveVulkan`](#renderer-perspectivevulkan-f32-f32-f32-f32) (depth 0..1).

The G-buffer geometry pass: draws every visible object once into the G-buffer with its material, depth-tested.

The G-buffer holds three color targets and a depth target (renderer spec R11): albedo (`RGBA8_SRGB`: rgb albedo, a baked occlusion), the world-space normal (`RG16_Float`, octahedral-encoded by [`renderer::OctEncode`](#renderer-octencode-const-vec3-amp)), the material (`RGBA8_UNorm`: roughness, metallic, emissive scale, flags / 255) and `D32_Float` depth (0 at the near plane, 1 at the far plane, no reverse-Z). The shaders are `gbuffer.vert` and `gbuffer.frag`; the fragment shader has permutations for a normal map, an alpha test and per-vertex colors, and [`renderer::CreateGBufferMaterial`](#renderer-creategbuffermaterial-materialsystem-star-const-gbuffertemplates-amp-const-gbufferoptions-amp-const-char-star) picks the matching vertex and fragment modules so the two never disagree.

A frame uses [`renderer::GBufferPass`](#renderer-gbufferpass) in two steps: `Prepare` (on the frame's thread) culls the scene, sorts the visible objects by [`renderer::MakeDrawSortKey`](#renderer-makedrawsortkey-u32-u32-u32), writes their per-object data in that order and builds the draw list; `AddToGraph` declares the pass whose callback records it. The record step creates no object and issues a handful of RHI calls per draw: bind the pipeline and material group only when they change, one instanced draw per run of the same mesh and material.

The GPU copy of a glyph atlas: one 8-bit coverage texture per CPU page, kept up to date and rebuilt after a device reset.

Deferred lighting for directional, ambient and point light, and the tonemap that brings the result to the display.

[`renderer::LightingPass`](#renderer-lightingpass) reads the G-buffer ([`renderer::GBufferPass`](#renderer-gbufferpass)) and writes linear HDR light into an `RGBA16_Float` accumulation target. One fullscreen triangle sums the ambient term and every directional light: per pixel it rebuilds the world position from depth and the inverse view-projection, decodes the surface, and applies the lighting model in `lighting_common.slang` (Lambert diffuse, GGX specular, metallic-roughness). Background pixels (depth 1.0) are skipped. Then every visible point light is drawn as a light volume (see below), adding to the same target. [`renderer::TonemapPass`](#renderer-tonemappass) then applies an exposure and Narkowicz's ACES fit and writes the target, whose sRGB format does the final encoding.

Point lights. A point light has a position and a radius; its contribution is `color * intensity * PointLightAttenuation(distance, radius)` shaded by the same model with the light vector pointing at the position. The attenuation reaches exactly zero at the radius, so the sphere of that radius bounds the light. The light volume pass draws, for each point light that survives frustum culling, a sphere of triangles (generated in the vertex shader from the vertex index, [`renderer::kLightVolumeVertexCount`](#renderer-klightvolumevertexcount) vertices) with additive blending, front faces culled so that a camera inside the sphere still draws its far side, no depth test, and the scissor set to the light's screen bounds. Every draw uses one pipeline and one bind group; the light is picked by the instance index into the light storage buffer.

The functions below are the CPU twins of the shader code, used by tests and tools; the real-device tests compare them with the GPU output.

A material is a shader permutation plus a parameter block and textures, with the bind groups and pipelines it needs.

A [`renderer::MaterialSystem`](#renderer-materialsystem) holds templates (the shaders and render state) and materials (the parameter values and textures of one surface). Everything is validated against the shaders' reflection when it is created, and the system owns every bind group and pipeline, so a draw creates no object. Descriptor sets follow one convention: set 0 is the frame uniform ([`renderer::FrameData`](#renderer-framedata)), set 1 the material, set 2 the per-object storage buffer.

CPU-side geometry ([`renderer::MeshData`](#renderer-meshdata)), its GPU copy ([`renderer::GpuMesh`](#renderer-gpumesh)) and procedural shapes.

The render graph: passes declare what they read and write, and compiling turns that into an ordered, culled schedule.

Resources are virtual: a transient texture or buffer exists only as a descriptor until a later stage allocates it, and an import wraps something that outlives the frame (the swapchain image, a persistent target). A pass is a name, an execute callback (a function pointer and a context pointer, no `std::function`) and a list of accesses, each with a usage that fixes the resource state it needs. Declaring records data only; every mistake in a graph is reported by [`renderer::RenderGraph::Compile`](#renderer-rendergraph-compile) as a named message, never while executing.

Semantics follow declaration order: a read sees the most recent earlier write, and a write replaces it. From that the graph derives read-after-write, write-after-write and write-after-read edges, orders the passes topologically with the smallest declaration index first among those ready (so the order is stable), and drops passes whose results nothing uses. All storage comes from the arena the graph is given, so building and compiling a frame allocates nothing from the heap.

The scene a frame is built from: a fixed-capacity list of objects and lights with stable ids. The renderer draws from this list; programs that use the ECS fill it with `scene::MakeRenderSyncSystem` (module `engine/scene`), which mirrors entities into it and touches it only for what changed. The renderer's own tests build it directly.

Rendering settings the user can change at run time, what each change costs, and how two sets of settings become the cheapest sufficient action.

A setting is declared once in a [`renderer::SettingsRegistry`](#renderer-settingsregistry) with a type, a default, a range, a [`renderer::CostClass`](#renderer-costclass) and the capability it needs. A [`renderer::RenderSettings`](#renderer-rendersettings) holds one value per registered setting. [`renderer::DiffSettings`](#renderer-diffsettings-const-rendersettings-amp-const-rendersettings-amp-const-capabilities-amp) compares two of them and reports the highest cost class among the changes, which is the cheapest action that is sufficient (a higher class includes the work of the lower ones). The registry is open: a later subsystem registers its own settings and takes part in the diff without any change here. Settings can be written to and read from a small text format.

The renderer's frame loop: begins a frame, builds and executes the render graph, and presents.

A [`renderer::Renderer`](#renderer-renderer) owns what lives across frames (the transient pool, the per-frame arena) and borrows the device, the optional swapchain and the optional job scheduler. A frame is built by a callback that declares the passes on a fresh [`renderer::RenderGraph`](#renderer-rendergraph); the renderer then compiles it, records the passes (in parallel when it has a scheduler), submits once and presents. Later days supply the real callback (G-buffer, lighting, tonemap); tests supply their own.

Identification of the renderer library; the first real symbol of Phase 5 (the camera and scene arrive on Day 73).

The renderer: a deferred, render-graph-driven renderer built on the RHI.

The frame of a lit scene: the material system, the per-frame buffers, the G-buffer, lighting and tonemap passes, an optional text overlay, and the [`Renderer`](#renderer-renderer) frame loop that runs them. One object per device. Moved here from the renderer demo on Phase 24 Day 359 so the C API's surfaces draw worlds with it; the demo and the tests use it too.

Shader permutations: one Slang source built offline into several SPIR-V modules, one per option combination.

A shader declares its options in a `// permutation-options: A B C` comment and uses them as compile-time defines (`#if A`); a manifest next to it (`<name>.perm`) lists which combinations to build. The `shader_mint` tool compiles each listed key, drops duplicate modules, and commits the result as `<name>.perm.inc`: a [`renderer::ShaderPermutations`](#renderer-shaderpermutations) table that [`renderer::FindPermutation`](#renderer-findpermutation-const-shaderpermutations-amp-permutationkey-reflectionerror-star) searches at run time. A key that was not built is an error naming the shader and the key, never a silent fallback.

What a compiled shader says about itself: its stage, bindings, uniform and push-constant block layouts, vertex inputs and compute local size. The offline `shader_mint` tool extracts it from `slangc` output into a compact blob committed as `shaders/<name>.refl.inc`; the renderer parses the blob and builds bind group layouts and vertex layouts from it instead of writing them a second time by hand.

The rendering settings in the engine-wide settings system.

[`renderer::RegisterSharedSettings`](#renderer-registersharedsettings-engine-placeholder-settings-registry-star) declares the built-in rendering settings in a [`settings::Registry`](foundation-settings.md#settings-registry) under the `renderer.` section (`renderer.present_mode`, `renderer.width`, ...), with the types, defaults, ranges and range policies of the renderer's own registry and each setting's [`renderer::CostClass`](#renderer-costclass) as its apply class. A [`settings::Settings`](foundation-settings.md#settings-settings) then loads, layers, validates and saves them, and [`renderer::ApplySharedChange`](#renderer-applysharedchange-void-star-const-engine-placeholder-settings-changeevent-amp) is the apply function that hands a change to [`renderer::Renderer::ApplySettings`](#renderer-renderer-applysettings-const-rendersettings-amp).

The renderer's own [`renderer::RenderSettings`](#renderer-rendersettings) stays the value type the renderer works with; this header converts to and from the shared system. Files written before the shared system (flat keys, `version = 1`) load through the schema-1 migration.

Draws laid-out text as textured quads in pixel coordinates, from a glyph atlas, with its own pipeline.

Textures and samplers that keep their source, so they can be made again after the device is replaced.

The transient pool: textures and buffers a frame's render graph needs, kept between frames and reused.

A graph declares a transient resource by description only. When the graph runs it takes a real texture or buffer with an equal description from this pool, or creates one, and gives it back afterwards, so a steady-state frame creates nothing. A pooled resource also remembers the state it was left in, because the next user starts from there. One that nobody asks for during a number of frames is destroyed.

Draws a [`ui::DrawList`](ui.md#ui-drawlist) on a colour target with one render-graph pass (Phase 15, Day 190).

The texture a UI surface is drawn into so the 3D world can show it (Phase 15, Day 202): a screen on a wall, a monitor, a floating panel.

The offscreen target of a UI viewport (Phase 15, Day 201): the colour and depth textures the world is drawn into, shown by the UI pass as an image.

## `PerspectiveVulkan(f32,f32,f32,f32)` {#renderer-perspectivevulkan-f32-f32-f32-f32}

`function renderer::PerspectiveVulkan` — defined in `engine/renderer/camera.h`

```cpp
Mat4 PerspectiveVulkan(f32 fov_y, f32 aspect, f32 near_plane, f32 far_plane);
```

A perspective projection for a right-handed view space and Vulkan clip space (Y down, depth 0..1).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `fov_y` | vertical field of view in radians, in (0, pi). |
| Parameter `aspect` | width / height, greater than zero. |
| Parameter `near_plane` | distance to the near plane, greater than zero. |
| Parameter `far_plane` | distance to the far plane, greater than `near_plane`. |
| Returns | the projection matrix. |
| Aborts | any argument is outside the range above. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |

A view matrix for a camera at `eye` looking at `target`, with `up` as the approximate up direction.

|---|---|

| Returns | the normal matrix; normalize transformed normals in the shader. |
| Fails | never. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its argument. |

## `Camera` {#renderer-camera}

`struct renderer::Camera` — defined in `engine/renderer/camera.h`

```cpp
struct Camera { ... }
```

A perspective camera: a position, an orientation and the projection parameters.

The orientation is an orthonormal basis stored as three columns: right, up and back (the camera looks along minus back). Everything else is derived, so a camera never holds a matrix that can go stale.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible plain data. |

## `Recreatable` {#renderer-recreatable}

`class renderer::Recreatable` — defined in `engine/renderer/device_resources.h`

```cpp
class Recreatable { ... }
```

An object that owns device objects and keeps what it needs to make them again.

The contract: `Release` destroys every device object and keeps the CPU-side source; `Recreate` makes them again on a device and re-uploads; neither caches a handle past a frame for anyone else. Both are called on the owning thread, with the device idle.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |

### `~Recreatable()` {#renderer-recreatable-tilde-recreatable}

`destructor renderer::Recreatable::~Recreatable` — defined in `engine/renderer/device_resources.h`

```cpp
virtual ~Recreatable() = default;
```

_Defaulted._

### `Release()` {#renderer-recreatable-release}

`method renderer::Recreatable::Release` — defined in `engine/renderer/device_resources.h`

```cpp
virtual void Release() = 0;
```

Destroys every device object this owner made; keeps the CPU-side source. Safe to call when already released.

### `Recreate(rhi::Device*)` {#renderer-recreatable-recreate-rhi-device-star}

`method renderer::Recreatable::Recreate` — defined in `engine/renderer/device_resources.h`

```cpp
virtual bool Recreate(rhi::Device* device) = 0;
```

Makes the device objects again.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the new device. |
| Returns | true on success; on failure nothing stays allocated. |
| Fails | returns false, logging the reason, if the source is gone or the device cannot hold the object (limits, memory). |
| Ownership | the device is borrowed; it outlives the objects until the next `Release`. |

Returns whether the owner currently holds its device objects. The registry asks when the owner registers, so an owner that registers after it made its objects is released at the next reset.

## `DeviceResources` {#renderer-deviceresources}

`class renderer::DeviceResources` — defined in `engine/renderer/device_resources.h`

```cpp

```

Rebuilds registered owners in tier order whenever the device host replaces the device.

Register it with [`rhi::DeviceHost::AddObserver`](rhi.md#rhi-devicehost-addobserver-deviceobserver-star-u32); `OnDeviceReleasing` releases owners from the highest tier down, `OnDeviceCreated` recreates them from the lowest up. A failed `Recreate` stops the sequence, releases everything that was built (a half-built set is never used), remembers the owner and returns false so the host fails the reset (the caller rolls back). Calling it again starts from released.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | borrows the owners; an owner must unregister (see [`renderer::ScopedRegistration`](#renderer-scopedregistration)) before it is destroyed. The registry must outlive every registration. |

```cpp
static constexpr usize kMaxOwners;
```

The most owners.

### `Register(Recreatable*,u32)` {#renderer-deviceresources-register-recreatable-star-u32}

`method renderer::DeviceResources::Register` — defined in `engine/renderer/device_resources.h`

```cpp
bool Register(Recreatable* owner, u32 tier);
```

Registers an owner.

The owner counts as live if `Live()` says it holds its objects now; one that does not is recreated at the next `OnDeviceCreated`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `owner` | borrowed; must stay alive until `Unregister`. |
| Parameter `tier` | its order, see the `tiers` namespace. |
| Returns | true if registered. |
| Fails | returns false, logging the reason, if the table is full or a reset is running. |
| Ownership | borrowed for the call; nothing is kept unless the description above says so. |

### `Unregister(Recreatable*)` {#renderer-deviceresources-unregister-recreatable-star}

`method renderer::DeviceResources::Unregister` — defined in `engine/renderer/device_resources.h`

```cpp
void Unregister(Recreatable* owner);
```

Removes an owner; unknown owners are ignored. Does not release the owner's objects: the owner does that itself. During a reset the entry is only marked dead and removed when the reset step ends, so an owner may unregister itself or another from inside `Release` or `Recreate`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Ownership | borrowed for the call; nothing is kept unless the description above says so. |

## `ScopedRegistration` {#renderer-scopedregistration}

`class renderer::ScopedRegistration` — defined in `engine/renderer/device_resources.h`

```cpp
class ScopedRegistration { ... }
```

Keeps an owner registered for exactly as long as this object lives.

Put one in each owner. It moves with the owner (call `Rebind` from the owner's move operations) and unregisters in its destructor, so a destroyed owner never gets a callback.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | borrows the registry and the owner. |

## `FrameResources` {#renderer-frameresources}

`class renderer::FrameResources` — defined in `engine/renderer/frame_data.h`

```cpp
class FrameResources final : public Recreatable { ... }
```

The per-frame buffers, in `kSlots` rotating regions so the CPU never writes data the GPU is still reading.

Call [`renderer::FrameResources::BeginFrame`](#renderer-frameresources-beginframe-u64) after `Device::BeginFrame`, write the frame block, the objects and the lights, then bind the regions. The buffers are persistently mapped `CpuToGpu` memory; they are flushed for devices whose memory is not coherent by [`renderer::FrameResources::Flush`](#renderer-frameresources-flush).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible. Not copyable. |
| Ownership | owns its buffers; the device must outlive it. |

### `kSlots` {#renderer-frameresources-kslots}

`variable renderer::FrameResources::kSlots` — defined in `engine/renderer/frame_data.h`

```cpp
static constexpr u32 kSlots;
```

The number of rotating regions; the largest frames in flight a device allows.

### `FrameResources(rhi::Device*,u32,u32,u32)` {#renderer-frameresources-frameresources-rhi-device-star-u32-u32-u32}

`constructor renderer::FrameResources::FrameResources` — defined in `engine/renderer/frame_data.h`

```cpp
FrameResources(rhi::Device* device, u32 max_objects, u32 max_lights = 64, u32 max_joint_matrices = 1024);
```

Creates the buffers.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the device; must outlive this object. |
| Parameter `max_objects` | the most objects one frame writes, at least 1. |
| Parameter `max_lights` | the most lights one frame writes, from 1 to [`renderer::kMaxLights`](#renderer-kmaxlights). |
| Parameter `max_joint_matrices` | the joint palette's room per frame, in 48-byte matrices, from 1 to [`renderer::kMaxJointMatrices`](#renderer-kmaxjointmatrices) (the setting `renderer.joint_palette_bytes` divided by 48). |
| Aborts | `device` is null, `max_objects` is 0, `max_lights` is 0 or above [`renderer::kMaxLights`](#renderer-kmaxlights), or `max_joint_matrices` is 0 or above [`renderer::kMaxJointMatrices`](#renderer-kmaxjointmatrices). |
| Ownership | check [`renderer::FrameResources::Valid`](#renderer-frameresources-valid-const). |

```cpp
u32 WriteObjects(const RenderScene& scene, const u32* slots, u32 count, const u32* joint_offsets = nullptr);
```

Writes one [`renderer::ObjectData`](#renderer-objectdata) per listed object, in list order: draw `i` uses instance index `i`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `scene` | the scene. |
| Parameter `slots` | object slots, as [`renderer::RenderScene::Cull`](#renderer-renderscene-cull-const-frustum-amp-u32-star-u32-const) returns them; each must be live. |
| Parameter `count` | number of slots; at most the capacity given at construction. |
| Returns | the number written (`count`). |
| Aborts | `BeginFrame` was not called, `count` exceeds the capacity, or a slot is dead. |
| Ownership | `slots` is borrowed for the call. |

### `WriteJoints(const f32*,u32)` {#renderer-frameresources-writejoints-const-f32-star-u32}

`method renderer::FrameResources::WriteJoints` — defined in `engine/renderer/frame_data.h`

```cpp
u32 WriteJoints(const f32* matrices, u32 count);
```

Appends one skinned instance's joint matrices to this frame's palette.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `matrices` | `count` row-major 3x4 matrices (12 `f32` each), copied. |
| Parameter `count` | from 1 to the remaining room. |
| Returns | the index of the first matrix (the offset to give [`renderer::FrameResources::WriteObjects`](#renderer-frameresources-writeobjects-const-renderscene-amp-const-u32-star-u32-const-u32-star)), or [`renderer::kNoJoints`](#renderer-knojoints) when the palette does not have `count` matrices left (nothing is written). |
| Aborts | `BeginFrame` was not called, or `matrices` is null. |
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible; call from the thread that prepares the frame. |

### `WriteLights(const RenderScene&,const FrameData&)` {#renderer-frameresources-writelights-const-renderscene-amp-const-framedata-amp}

`method renderer::FrameResources::WriteLights` — defined in `engine/renderer/frame_data.h`

```cpp
LightSummary WriteLights(const RenderScene& scene, const FrameData& view);
```

Writes the scene's lights into this frame's region: directional lights first, then the visible point lights, and sums the ambient lights.

Directional light directions are normalized (a zero direction lights nothing). A point light whose sphere (position, radius) is entirely outside the view frustum is left out ([`renderer::LightSummary::point_culled`](#renderer-lightsummary-point-culled)); the rest are sorted by squared distance from the camera to the light position, nearest first, ties broken by the light's slot, so the order is the same on every run. If the lights do not fit in `max_lights`, directional lights are written first and the farthest point lights are left out; the number left out is [`renderer::LightSummary::dropped`](#renderer-lightsummary-dropped) and an error is logged (once per object). The capacity should be at least the scene's `RenderSceneDesc::max_lights`, which makes that impossible.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `scene` | the scene. |
| Parameter `view` | the frame block of the view being lit; its `view_projection` and `camera_position` are used. |
| Returns | the ambient sum, the counts written and the counts left out. |
| Aborts | [`renderer::FrameResources::BeginFrame`](#renderer-frameresources-beginframe-u64) was not called. |

`method renderer::FrameResources::WrittenLights` — defined in `engine/renderer/frame_data.h`

```cpp
const LightData* WrittenLights() const;
```

Returns the lights written by the last `WriteLights`, in buffer order.

It is a CPU copy, because the mapped buffer is write-only memory. Valid until the next `BeginFrame`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | never. |
| Ownership | borrowed. |

## `CheckGBufferFormats(const rhi::Device*,char*,usize)` {#renderer-checkgbufferformats-const-rhi-device-star-char-star-usize}

`function renderer::CheckGBufferFormats` — defined in `engine/renderer/gbuffer.h`

```cpp
bool CheckGBufferFormats(const rhi::Device* device, char* error, usize error_capacity);
```

Checks that the device supports every G-buffer format for the usage the pass needs, and enough simultaneous color attachments (the geometry pass writes three). Call it once at start-up, before creating templates and passes: the passes themselves do not repeat it, and on a device that fails it every object would be left out with a log line per pipeline.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the device to ask. |
| Parameter `error` | receives a message naming the first unsupported format and usage, or the attachment limit; may be null. |
| Parameter `error_capacity` | size of `error` in bytes. |
| Returns | true when all formats are supported. |
| Fails | returns false and fills `error`; nothing is logged or aborted. |
| Ownership | `error` is borrowed for the call. |

## `FitCameraDepthRange(const RenderScene&,Camera*,f32)` {#renderer-fitcameradepthrange-const-renderscene-amp-camera-star-f32}

`function renderer::FitCameraDepthRange` — defined in `engine/renderer/gbuffer.h`

```cpp
void FitCameraDepthRange(const RenderScene& scene, Camera* camera, f32 min_near);
```

Sets a camera's near and far planes to enclose every visible-or-not object of the scene.

The planes come from the world-space bounding spheres projected on the camera's forward axis, with the near plane never below `min_near` and the far plane at least twice the near one. A scene without objects leaves the camera as it was.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `scene` | the scene. |
| Parameter `camera` | the camera to change; its position and rotation must be set. |
| Parameter `min_near` | the smallest near distance allowed, positive. |
| Aborts | `camera` is null or `min_near` is not positive. |
| Ownership | `camera` is borrowed for the call. |

## `CreateGBufferMaterial(MaterialSystem*,const GBufferTemplates&,const GBufferOptions&,const char*)` {#renderer-creategbuffermaterial-materialsystem-star-const-gbuffertemplates-amp-const-gbufferoptions-amp-const-char-star}

`function renderer::CreateGBufferMaterial` — defined in `engine/renderer/gbuffer.h`

```cpp
MaterialId CreateGBufferMaterial(MaterialSystem* materials, const GBufferTemplates& templates, const GBufferOptions& options, const char* name);
```

Creates a G-buffer material with the options' shader modules and sensible parameters.

The parameters start as base color white, roughness 0.5, metallic 0, emissive scale 0, alpha cutoff 0.5 and occlusion 1; change them with the material system's setters (`base_color`, `roughness`, `metallic`, `emissive_scale`, `alpha_cutoff`, `occlusion`, `flags`), and the textures `albedo_tex` and `normal_tex`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `materials` | the system. |
| Parameter `templates` | from [`renderer::CreateGBufferTemplates`](#renderer-creategbuffertemplates-materialsystem-star). |
| Parameter `options` | which permutation to use. |
| Parameter `name` | a debug name; may be null. |
| Returns | the material, or an invalid id when the template is invalid or the permutation does not exist (logged). |
| Fails | reported through the invalid id. |
| Aborts | `materials` is null. |
| Ownership | the material belongs to `materials`. |

### `Prepare(const RenderScene&,const Frustum&)` {#renderer-gbufferpass-prepare-const-renderscene-amp-const-frustum-amp}

`method renderer::GBufferPass::Prepare` — defined in `engine/renderer/gbuffer.h`

```cpp
bool Prepare(const RenderScene& scene, const Frustum& frustum);
```

Culls the scene, sorts the visible objects and writes their per-object data.

Call it after `FrameResources::BeginFrame` and `MaterialSystem::BeginFrame`/`FlushMaterials` and before `FrameResources::Flush`. The objects are sorted by [`renderer::MakeDrawSortKey`](#renderer-makedrawsortkey-u32-u32-u32) (pipeline, material, mesh) with the scene slot as tie-break, so the result does not depend on the order objects were added. An object is left out and named in `LastError` when its mesh is missing or not prepared, its material does not exist, a pipeline cannot be built for the mesh, or the mesh and the material disagree about per-vertex colors.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `scene` | the scene. |
| Parameter `frustum` | the camera's frustum. |
| Returns | true when every visible object was accepted. |
| Fails | returns false when any object was rejected (the rest is still drawn); the first message is in `LastError`. |
| Ownership | `scene` is borrowed until the pass has been recorded. |

### `AddToGraph(RenderGraph*,u32,u32)` {#renderer-gbufferpass-addtograph-rendergraph-star-u32-u32}

| Parameter `width` | the target width in pixels. |
| Parameter `height` | the target height in pixels. |
| Returns | the targets; the pass writes all four. |
| Aborts | called twice since the last `Prepare` (one pass object serves one view per frame; make one per view). |
| Ownership | `graph` is borrowed for the call; the pass keeps a pointer to this object until the graph is executed. |


| Ownership | the pass keeps a pointer to this object until the graph is executed. |

## `GlyphAtlasTexture` {#renderer-glyphatlastexture}

`class renderer::GlyphAtlasTexture` — defined in `engine/renderer/glyph_atlas_texture.h`

```cpp
class GlyphAtlasTexture final : public Recreatable { ... }
```

One R8 texture for each page of a [`text::GlyphAtlas`](text.md#text-glyphatlas).

The atlas (CPU side) is the source of truth: [`renderer::GlyphAtlasTexture::Sync`](#renderer-glyphatlastexture-sync) creates a texture for each page the atlas has gained and re-sends a page when the atlas reports that it changed. A changed page is sent whole, because the RHI uploads a full mip level at a time (a sub-rectangle upload is on `KNOWN-ISSUES.md`); with the default 1024-pixel pages that is one megabyte per changed page per frame at most, and nothing for a page that did not change. The object registers with a [`renderer::DeviceResources`](#renderer-deviceresources) at the texture tier; after a reset `Recreate` makes every page again from the atlas, so no glyph is lost. The handles change then: read [`renderer::GlyphAtlasTexture::Page`](#renderer-glyphatlastexture-page-u32-const) each frame, never keep one.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only, and not safe to use while another thread adds glyphs to the atlas. |
| Ownership | borrows the atlas, which must outlive this object; owns the textures. The device and the registry must outlive it. |


Brings the GPU pages up to date: creates textures for new pages and sends every page that changed since the last call.

| Aborts | the object does not hold its textures (it was never created, or the device was released and not yet recreated). |

## `ShadeLight(const SurfacePoint&,const Vec3&,const Vec3&,const Vec3&)` {#renderer-shadelight-const-surfacepoint-amp-const-vec3-amp-const-vec3-amp-const-vec3-amp}

`function renderer::ShadeLight` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp
Vec3 ShadeLight(const SurfacePoint& surface, const Vec3& view, const Vec3& light, const Vec3& radiance);
```

Returns one light's contribution to a surface: `(diffuse + specular) * radiance * max(dot(N, L), 0)`.

The diffuse BRDF is the albedo times `1 - metallic` (the 1/pi of a Lambertian surface is folded into the intensity) and the specular lobe is GGX with height-correlated Smith visibility and Schlick Fresnel, multiplied by pi to match. The exact formulas are in `lighting_common.slang`, which this mirrors.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `surface` | the decoded surface. |
| Parameter `view` | unit vector from the surface toward the viewer. |
| Parameter `light` | unit vector from the surface toward the light. |
| Parameter `radiance` | the light's color times its intensity (linear). |
| Returns | the linear RGB contribution; zero when the surface faces away from the light. |
| Fails | never. |

## `ShadeAmbient(const SurfacePoint&,const Vec3&)` {#renderer-shadeambient-const-surfacepoint-amp-const-vec3-amp}

`function renderer::ShadeAmbient` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp
Vec3 ShadeAmbient(const SurfacePoint& surface, const Vec3& ambient);
```

Returns the ambient and emissive terms: `albedo * (1 - metallic) * ambient * occlusion + albedo * emissive_scale`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `surface` | the decoded surface. |
| Parameter `ambient` | the summed ambient light (linear). |
| Returns | the linear RGB contribution. |
| Fails | never. |

## `TonemapAces(f32)` {#renderer-tonemapaces-f32}

`function renderer::TonemapAces` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp
f32 TonemapAces(f32 x);
```

Applies Narkowicz's ACES fit to one linear channel: `clamp(x (2.51 x + 0.03) / (x (2.43 x + 0.59) + 0.14), 0, 1)`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `x` | the channel after exposure, not negative. |
| Returns | the display-linear value in [0, 1] (the sRGB format of the target encodes it). |
| Fails | never. |

## `ReconstructWorldPosition(u32,u32,f32,u32,u32,const Mat4&)` {#renderer-reconstructworldposition-u32-u32-f32-u32-u32-const-mat4-amp}

`function renderer::ReconstructWorldPosition` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp
Vec3 ReconstructWorldPosition(u32 pixel_x, u32 pixel_y, f32 depth, u32 width, u32 height, const Mat4& inverse_view_projection);
```

Rebuilds a world position from a depth-buffer value, as the lighting shader does.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `pixel_x` | the pixel column; the pixel center, `pixel_x + 0.5`, is used. |
| Parameter `pixel_y` | the pixel row (0 at the top). |
| Parameter `depth` | the depth value in [0, 1]. |
| Parameter `width` | the target width in pixels. |
| Parameter `height` | the target height in pixels. |
| Parameter `inverse_view_projection` | [`renderer::FrameData::inverse_view_projection`](#renderer-framedata-inverse-view-projection) (it already undoes the projection's Y flip). |
| Returns | the world-space position. |
| Fails | never. |

## `PointLightAttenuation(f32,f32)` {#renderer-pointlightattenuation-f32-f32}

`function renderer::PointLightAttenuation` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp
f32 PointLightAttenuation(f32 distance, f32 radius);
```

Returns a point light's attenuation at a distance: `saturate(1 - (d / R)^4)^2 / (d^2 + 1)` with `d` the distance and `R` the radius.

It is 1 at the light, falls like an inverse square (the `+ 1` keeps it finite) over most of the range, and is smoothly windowed to exactly 0 at `d = R` with zero slope there, staying 0 beyond. The shader multiplies the light's radiance by it. The CPU twin of `PointAttenuation` in `lighting_common.slang`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `distance` | the distance from the light, not negative. |
| Parameter `radius` | the light's radius, greater than zero. |
| Returns | a factor in [0, 1]. |
| Fails | never. |

## `kLightVolumeSegments` {#renderer-klightvolumesegments}

`variable renderer::kLightVolumeSegments` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp
inline constexpr u32 kLightVolumeSegments;
```

`variable renderer::kLightVolumeRings` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp

```

Bands of the light volume sphere from pole to pole.

## `kLightVolumeVertexCount` {#renderer-klightvolumevertexcount}

`variable renderer::kLightVolumeVertexCount` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp
inline constexpr u32 kLightVolumeVertexCount;
```

Vertices of one light volume: two triangles per quad, `kLightVolumeSegments * kLightVolumeRings` quads.

## `LightVolumeScale()` {#renderer-lightvolumescale}

`function renderer::LightVolumeScale` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp
f32 LightVolumeScale();
```

Returns the factor that enlarges the light volume's unit sphere so it contains the light's sphere.

Every face then lies at least one radius from the center: the factor is `1 / (cos(pi / segments) cos(pi / rings))`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | never. |

## `LightVolumeVertex(u32)` {#renderer-lightvolumevertex-u32}

`function renderer::LightVolumeVertex` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp
Vec3 LightVolumeVertex(u32 index);
```

Returns vertex `index` of the light volume, relative to the light's position, in units of its radius, scale included.

The CPU twin of the vertex generation in `point_light.vert`. Triangles are counter-clockwise seen from outside.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `index` | from 0 to `kLightVolumeVertexCount - 1`. |
| Returns | the offset from the light's position divided by its radius. |
| Aborts | `index` is out of range. |

## `LightScreenRect(const FrameData&,const Vec3&,f32,u32,u32,rhi::ScissorRect*)` {#renderer-lightscreenrect-const-framedata-amp-const-vec3-amp-f32-u32-u32-rhi-scissorrect-star}

`function renderer::LightScreenRect` — defined in `engine/renderer/lighting.h`

```cpp
bool LightScreenRect(const FrameData& view, const Vec3& center, f32 radius, u32 width, u32 height, rhi::ScissorRect* out);
```

Computes the pixel rectangle that bounds a point light's sphere on screen, for the scissor.

A sphere that reaches the camera's plane (any corner of its bounding box is not in front of the camera) gets the whole target; otherwise the rectangle is the projection of the box, clamped to the target.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `view` | the frame block ([`renderer::FrameData::view_projection`](#renderer-framedata-view-projection) is used). |
| Parameter `center` | the light's position. |
| Parameter `radius` | the light's radius, greater than zero. |
| Parameter `width` | the target width in pixels. |
| Parameter `height` | the target height in pixels. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the rectangle (origin top-left, as the framebuffer); must not be null. |
| Returns | false when the rectangle is empty (the light cannot touch any pixel), true otherwise. |
| Fails | reported through the return value. |
| Aborts | `out` is null, the target size is zero or `radius` is not positive. |
| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call. |

Its pipelines are built only for skinned mesh layouts, and a rigid template only for rigid ones: [`renderer::MaterialSystem::GetPipeline`](#renderer-materialsystem-getpipeline-materialid-const-pipelinetarget-amp-const-vertexlayoutdata-amp-rhi-primitivetopology-bool) reports the mismatch.

## `MakeDrawSortKey(u32,u32,u32)` {#renderer-makedrawsortkey-u32-u32-u32}

`function renderer::MakeDrawSortKey` — defined in `engine/renderer/material.h`

```cpp
u64 MakeDrawSortKey(u32 pipeline_index, u32 material_index, u32 mesh_index);
```

Packs the draw order into one integer: pipeline first, then material, then mesh.

Sorting by this key groups draws that share a pipeline and, within it, a material, so pipeline and material bind groups change as rarely as possible.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `pipeline_index` | [`renderer::PipelineLookup::index`](#renderer-pipelinelookup-index); below 65536. |
| Parameter `material_index` | [`renderer::MaterialId::index`](#renderer-materialid-index); below 2^24. |
| Parameter `mesh_index` | any stable small number for the mesh (for example its slot); below 2^24. |
| Returns | the key; compare keys with `<`. |
| Aborts | an argument is out of range. |

## `MaterialSystem` {#renderer-materialsystem}

`class renderer::MaterialSystem` — defined in `engine/renderer/material.h`

```cpp
class MaterialSystem final : public Recreatable { ... }
```

Templates, materials, default textures, bind groups and the pipeline cache.

Creation validates against reflection and returns an invalid id (after logging) on any mismatch; a stale or invalid id passed to another function is a programming error. Parameter writes go to a CPU shadow and reach the GPU in one batch per frame in [`renderer::MaterialSystem::FlushMaterials`](#renderer-materialsystem-flushmaterials). Use is: `BeginFrame`, change materials, `FlushMaterials`, then record draws with the bind groups and pipelines the system hands out.

Parameter names are the member names of the fragment (or vertex) shader's set-1 uniform block; texture and sampler names are the shader's variable names. A texture slot left unset uses a default texture: the one the template lists for it in `texture_defaults`, otherwise one chosen by name (a name starting with `normal` gets the flat normal, one starting with `emissive` or `black` gets black, anything else white). Name your normal and emissive slots to match, or list them. Samplers left unset use a linear, repeating sampler.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible. |
| Ownership | borrows the device, which must outlive it; owns the shaders, layouts, buffers, bind groups, pipelines and default textures it creates. Textures and samplers given to a material belong to the caller and must outlive it. |

### `MaterialSystem(rhi::Device*,const MaterialSystemDesc&)` {#renderer-materialsystem-materialsystem-rhi-device-star-const-materialsystemdesc-amp}

`constructor renderer::MaterialSystem::MaterialSystem` — defined in `engine/renderer/material.h`

```cpp
MaterialSystem(rhi::Device* device, const MaterialSystemDesc& desc);
```

Creates the system, the frame and object layouts, the default textures and the default sampler.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the device; must not be null. |
| Parameter `desc` | capacities; each at least 1, at most 2^24 materials and 65536 pipelines (the draw sort key's range). |
| Fails | creating a default resource fails; [`renderer::MaterialSystem::Valid`](#renderer-materialsystem-valid-const) then returns false. |
| Aborts | `device` is null or a capacity is 0 or too large. |
| Ownership | `device` is borrowed. |

Destroys every material, template, pipeline and default resource. The GPU may still be using them; the device defers the actual release.

Creates a template.

### `CreateMaterial(TemplateId,PermutationKey,const char*)` {#renderer-materialsystem-creatematerial-templateid-permutationkey-const-char-star}

`method renderer::MaterialSystem::CreateMaterial` — defined in `engine/renderer/material.h`

```cpp
MaterialId CreateMaterial(TemplateId tmpl, PermutationKey key, const char* name);
```

Creates a material.

The set-1 bindings of the chosen fragment module and the vertex module are merged into a layout; the parameter block starts zeroed and every texture and sampler slot starts on its default.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `tmpl` | a live template. |
| Parameter `key` | the fragment permutation; ignored (use the default key) for a template without a permuted fragment. |
| Parameter `name` | debug name, at most 47 characters; may be null. |
| Returns | the material, or an invalid id after logging the reason. |
| Fails | the key was not built, the fragment shader breaks the set conventions, the set-1 bindings do not merge, the parameter block exceeds [`renderer::kMaxMaterialParamBytes`](#renderer-kmaxmaterialparambytes), there are more textures or samplers than the limits allow, a binding is an array or an unsupported type, the vertex and fragment shaders declare different parameter blocks, or the material list is full or a GPU object could not be created. |
| Aborts | `tmpl` is invalid or stale. |
| Ownership | `name` is copied. |

### `FrameBindGroup(const BufferRegion&)` {#renderer-materialsystem-framebindgroup-const-bufferregion-amp}

`method renderer::MaterialSystem::FrameBindGroup` — defined in `engine/renderer/material.h`

```cpp
rhi::BindGroupHandle FrameBindGroup(const BufferRegion& region);
```

Returns the bind group that binds `region` of a buffer as the set-0 frame uniform ([`renderer::FrameData`](#renderer-framedata)).

The group is created on first use and cached by buffer, offset and size, so the three regions of a [`renderer::FrameResources`](#renderer-frameresources) cost three creations in total. The system holds up to 8 such groups; past that the oldest is destroyed (through the device's deferred destruction), so recreating the frame resources does not exhaust it.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `region` | the frame region from [`renderer::FrameResources::FrameRegion`](#renderer-frameresources-frameregion-const). |
| Returns | the group, or an invalid handle when the device cannot create it (logged). |
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible; call from one thread (the one preparing the frame). |
| Ownership | the system owns the group; it lives until it is evicted or the system is destroyed, which is fine after the buffer itself was destroyed. |

### `AddObjectIndexAttribute(const rhi::Device&,rhi::VertexBindingDesc*,u32*,u32,rhi::VertexAttributeDesc*,u32*,u32,u32)` {#renderer-materialsystem-addobjectindexattribute-const-rhi-device-amp-rhi-vertexbindingdesc-star-u32-star-u32-rhi-vertexattributedesc-star-u32-star-u32-u32}

`method renderer::MaterialSystem::AddObjectIndexAttribute` — defined in `engine/renderer/material.h`

```cpp
static bool AddObjectIndexAttribute(const rhi::Device& device, rhi::VertexBindingDesc* bindings, u32* binding_count, u32 binding_capacity, rhi::VertexAttributeDesc* attributes, u32* attribute_count, u32 attribute_capacity, u32 slot);
```

Adds the object index attribute to a pipeline's vertex input when the device needs it: binding `slot`, instance rate, 4-byte stride, attribute location [`renderer::kObjectIndexLocation`](#renderer-kobjectindexlocation) as `UInt1`. Does nothing when [`rhi::DeviceCaps::object_index_stream`](rhi.md#rhi-devicecaps-object-index-stream) is false.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the device the pipeline is for. |
| Parameter `bindings` | the pipeline's vertex bindings; `*binding_count` of them are used, and one is appended at index `slot`. |
| Parameter `binding_count` | in and out. |
| Parameter `binding_capacity` | the size of `bindings`. |
| Parameter `attributes` | the pipeline's attributes; `*attribute_count` used, one appended. |
| Parameter `attribute_count` | in and out. |
| Parameter `attribute_capacity` | the size of `attributes`. |
| Parameter `slot` | the vertex-buffer slot, which must equal `*binding_count` (bindings are dense). |
| Returns | true if the attribute was added or not needed. |
| Fails | returns false when `slot` is not the next binding or an array is full. |
| Ownership | the arrays belong to the caller. |

### `GetPipeline(MaterialId,const PipelineTarget&,const VertexLayoutData&,rhi::PrimitiveTopology,bool)` {#renderer-materialsystem-getpipeline-materialid-const-pipelinetarget-amp-const-vertexlayoutdata-amp-rhi-primitivetopology-bool}

`method renderer::MaterialSystem::GetPipeline` — defined in `engine/renderer/material.h`

```cpp
PipelineLookup GetPipeline(MaterialId id, const PipelineTarget& target, const VertexLayoutData& vertex_layout, rhi::PrimitiveTopology topology = rhi::PrimitiveTopology::TriangleList, bool primitive_restart = false);
```

Returns the pipeline a material draws with into a target and with a vertex layout, creating it on a miss.

Pipelines are keyed by (template, permutation, target, vertex layout, topology, restart), so materials of one template and key share one; a strip mesh and a list mesh with the same material get separate pipelines. Creation happens here, never while recording: call this (or [`renderer::MaterialSystem::Warmup`](#renderer-materialsystem-warmup-materialid-const-pipelinetarget-amp-const-vertexlayoutdata-amp-rhi-primitivetopology-bool)) before the frame's recording starts.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `id` | a live material. |
| Parameter `target` | the attachment formats. |
| Parameter `vertex_layout` | the mesh layout; every vertex input of the vertex shader must be present with the same format. |
| Parameter `topology` | the mesh's topology ([`renderer::MeshData::Topology`](#renderer-meshdata-topology-const)); a triangle list by default. |
| Parameter `primitive_restart` | whether the mesh has restart markers ([`renderer::MeshData::HasPrimitiveRestart`](#renderer-meshdata-hasprimitiverestart-const)). |
| Returns | the pipeline and its cache index, or an invalid pipeline after logging. |
| Fails | the layout lacks or mismatches a vertex shader input, the target is invalid, restart is asked for with a topology that is not a strip, the cache is full, or the device cannot create the pipeline. |
| Aborts | `id` is invalid or stale. |

### `Warmup(MaterialId,const PipelineTarget&,const VertexLayoutData&,rhi::PrimitiveTopology,bool)` {#renderer-materialsystem-warmup-materialid-const-pipelinetarget-amp-const-vertexlayoutdata-amp-rhi-primitivetopology-bool}

| Aborts | `id` is invalid or stale. |

### `Release()` {#renderer-materialsystem-release}

`method renderer::MaterialSystem::Release` — defined in `engine/renderer/material.h`

```cpp
void Release() override;
```

Destroys every device object (layouts, shaders, default textures, parameter buffers, bind groups, pipelines) and keeps the CPU side: template descriptors and reflection, parameter values, owner bindings, and the pipeline descriptors. Raw texture and sampler handles set by the caller are forgotten (they belong to the old device).

## `StandardVertex` {#renderer-standardvertex}

`struct renderer::StandardVertex` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
struct StandardVertex { ... }
```

The vertex the procedural meshes use: position, normal, tangent (xyz plus handedness in w) and one UV set. 48 bytes, interleaved in one stream.

The bitangent is `cross(normal, tangent.xyz) * tangent.w`. UV (0, 0) is the corner where the texture starts, and V grows along the bitangent.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible plain data. |

### `position` {#renderer-standardvertex-position}

`variable renderer::StandardVertex::position` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
f32 position[3];
```

Object-space position.

### `normal` {#renderer-standardvertex-normal}

`variable renderer::StandardVertex::normal` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
f32 normal[3];
```

Unit normal.

### `tangent` {#renderer-standardvertex-tangent}

`variable renderer::StandardVertex::tangent` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
f32 tangent[4];
```

Unit tangent in xyz, handedness (+1 or -1) in w.

### `uv` {#renderer-standardvertex-uv}

`variable renderer::StandardVertex::uv` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
f32 uv[2];
```

Texture coordinates.


## `ColorVertex` {#renderer-colorvertex}

`struct renderer::ColorVertex` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
struct ColorVertex { ... }
```

A [`renderer::StandardVertex`](#renderer-standardvertex) with a per-vertex color: the same fields plus four bytes of RGBA, 52 bytes, in the same interleaved stream.

The color is stored as authored sRGB bytes (not linear): the geometry shader converts it to linear light, so a color picked in an image editor reads as expected. Alpha is linear coverage. A material that does not declare the vertex-color permutation ignores the attribute; a shader that reads it needs a mesh that has it (the material system reports a mismatch when it builds the pipeline).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible plain data. |

### `base` {#renderer-colorvertex-base}

`variable renderer::ColorVertex::base` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
StandardVertex base;
```

The standard attributes.

### `color` {#renderer-colorvertex-color}

`variable renderer::ColorVertex::color` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
u8 color[4];
```

Red, green, blue, alpha, 0 to 255.


The layout of [`renderer::ColorVertex`](#renderer-colorvertex): the four standard attributes of [`renderer::StandardVertexLayout`](#renderer-standardvertexlayout) plus the color at location 4 (`UNorm8x4`, offset 48), all in vertex binding 0.

|---|---|

## `SkinnedVertexLayout(const VertexLayoutData&)` {#renderer-skinnedvertexlayout-const-vertexlayoutdata-amp}

`function renderer::SkinnedVertexLayout` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
VertexLayoutData SkinnedVertexLayout(const VertexLayoutData& base);
```

The skinned form of a mesh layout: `base` plus the skin stream in vertex binding 1, joint indices (`UInt8x4`) at location [`renderer::kSkinJointsLocation`](#renderer-kskinjointslocation) and weights (`UNorm8x4`) at [`renderer::kSkinWeightsLocation`](#renderer-kskinweightslocation), 8 bytes per vertex (`skin_stride`). Rigid layouts are unchanged.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `base` | a layout without a skin stream. |
| Returns | the layout. |
| Aborts | `base` already has a skin stream or has no room for two more attributes. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its argument. |

## `MeshGeometry` {#renderer-meshgeometry}

`struct renderer::MeshGeometry` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
struct MeshGeometry { ... }
```

Geometry read in place: what [`renderer::GpuMesh`](#renderer-gpumesh) uploads, and uploads again after a device reset, without copying a byte of it. A loaded mesh blob hands its sections over this way (the minter laid them out exactly as the buffers hold them). The bytes are not pointed at: the geometry holds a share of the region they are in and the offsets of the three sections, so the region lives as long as the mesh that uploads from it, whoever else lets go.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible plain data (the shared region is immutable). |
| Ownership | shares `bytes`. |

### `layout` {#renderer-meshgeometry-layout}

`variable renderer::MeshGeometry::layout` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`



Where the vertices start in `bytes`; `vertex_count * layout.stride` bytes.

At least 1.


Where the indices start in `bytes`.

At least 1; fits `topology`.

### `Validate()` {#renderer-meshgeometry-validate-const}

`method renderer::MeshGeometry::Validate` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
const char* Validate() const;
```

Checks the sections against the region and the counts against each other (not the values in the bytes).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | null when the geometry can be uploaded, otherwise a static message. |
| Fails | no region, a section outside it, a zero count or stride, an index count that does not fit the topology, a skinned layout without a joint count of 1 to 256 or with a stride other than 8, or a joint count with a rigid layout. |
| Ownership | reads the shared bytes only to learn their size; the message is static. |

## `MeshData` {#renderer-meshdata}

```cpp
class MeshData { ... }
```

Geometry on the CPU: interleaved vertex bytes, indices and bounds. Owns its memory.

A mesh is built once with [`renderer::MeshData::Set`](#renderer-meshdata-set-const-vertexlayoutdata-amp-u32-const-void-star-u32-rhi-indextype-const-void-star-u32-rhi-primitivetopology) (or by one of the `Make...Mesh` functions) and is read-only afterwards.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible. Move-only. |
| Ownership | owns copies of the vertex and index data it was given. |

### `Set(const VertexLayoutData&,u32,const void*,u32,rhi::IndexType,const void*,u32,rhi::PrimitiveTopology)` {#renderer-meshdata-set-const-vertexlayoutdata-amp-u32-const-void-star-u32-rhi-indextype-const-void-star-u32-rhi-primitivetopology}

`method renderer::MeshData::Set` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
const char* Set(const VertexLayoutData& layout, u32 position_offset, const void* vertices, u32 vertex_count, rhi::IndexType index_type, const void* indices, u32 index_count, rhi::PrimitiveTopology topology = rhi::PrimitiveTopology::TriangleList);
```

Replaces the contents with copies of the given geometry and computes its bounds.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `layout` | the vertex attributes; `layout.stride` is the byte size of one vertex. |
| Parameter `position_offset` | byte offset of the three-float position inside a vertex, used for the bounds. |
| Parameter `vertices` | `vertex_count * layout.stride` bytes. |
| Parameter `vertex_count` | number of vertices, at least 1. |
| Parameter `index_type` | 16-bit or 32-bit indices. |
| Parameter `indices` | `index_count` indices of `index_type`. |
| Parameter `index_count` | number of indices; for a list topology a positive multiple of the primitive size (3 for `TriangleList`, 2 for `LineList`), for a strip at least 3 (`TriangleStrip`) or 2 (`LineStrip`). |
| Parameter `topology` | how the indices form primitives; defaults to a triangle list. In the strip topologies an index with every bit set (0xFFFF or 0xFFFFFFFF) is a restart marker, not a vertex: it ends the strip and starts the next one (see [`renderer::MeshData::HasPrimitiveRestart`](#renderer-meshdata-hasprimitiverestart-const)). |
| Returns | null on success, otherwise a static message naming the problem (this mesh is then empty). |
| Fails | a zero or oversized stride, a stride or position offset that is not a multiple of 4, a position outside the vertex, no vertices or indices, an index count that does not fit the topology, 16-bit indices with more than 65536 vertices (65535 for a strip, whose last index value is the restart marker), an index at or past `vertex_count`, or a non-finite position. |
| Ownership | `vertices` and `indices` are borrowed for the call and copied. |

### `SetSkin(const void*,u32)` {#renderer-meshdata-setskin-const-void-star-u32}

`method renderer::MeshData::SetSkin` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
const char* SetSkin(const void* skin, u32 joint_count);
```

Adds the skin stream of a skinned mesh: eight bytes per vertex, joint indices (`UInt8x4`) then weights (`UNorm8x4`), the layout of the mesh blob's skin section. The mesh's layout then carries `skin_stride` 8 and the two skin attributes ([`renderer::SkinnedVertexLayout`](#renderer-skinnedvertexlayout-const-vertexlayoutdata-amp)). Call it after [`renderer::MeshData::Set`](#renderer-meshdata-set-const-vertexlayoutdata-amp-u32-const-void-star-u32-rhi-indextype-const-void-star-u32-rhi-primitivetopology); a later `Set` drops the skin stream.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `skin` | `VertexCount() * 8` bytes, copied. |
| Parameter `joint_count` | the skeleton's joint count; every index of a vertex with a nonzero weight must be below it. |
| Returns | null, or a message (the mesh is empty or already skinned, a weight does not sum to 255, a weighted joint is out of range, out of memory); on failure the mesh is unchanged. |
| Fails | reported through the return value. |
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible. |
| Ownership | the bytes are copied. |

## `MakeCubeMesh(f32,MeshData*)` {#renderer-makecubemesh-f32-meshdata-star}

`function renderer::MakeCubeMesh` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
const char* MakeCubeMesh(f32 edge, MeshData* out);
```

Builds an axis-aligned cube centered on the origin: 24 vertices (4 per face) and 36 16-bit indices, outward normals, counter-clockwise faces, UV (0, 0) to (1, 1) on every face.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `edge` | edge length, greater than zero. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the mesh; must not be null. |
| Returns | null on success, otherwise a static message. |
| Fails | `edge` is not positive and finite. |
| Aborts | `out` is null. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call; the message is static. |

## `MakeSphereMesh(f32,u32,u32,MeshData*)` {#renderer-makespheremesh-f32-u32-u32-meshdata-star}

`function renderer::MakeSphereMesh` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
const char* MakeSphereMesh(f32 radius, u32 segments, u32 rings, MeshData* out);
```

Builds a UV sphere centered on the origin, Y up: `(rings + 1) * (segments + 1)` vertices (the seam and the poles are duplicated for UVs), outward normals, counter-clockwise triangles, no degenerate triangles at the poles.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `radius` | greater than zero. |
| Parameter `segments` | slices around the Y axis, at least 3. |
| Parameter `rings` | bands from pole to pole, at least 2. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the mesh; must not be null. |
| Returns | null on success, otherwise a static message. |
| Fails | an argument out of range, or more vertices than 16-bit indices can address (then 32-bit indices are used, so this only fails for absurd sizes). |
| Aborts | `out` is null. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call; the message is static. |

## `MakePlaneMesh(f32,f32,u32,u32,MeshData*)` {#renderer-makeplanemesh-f32-f32-u32-u32-meshdata-star}

Builds a flat grid in the XZ plane centered on the origin, normal +Y, tangent +X, UV (0, 0) at (-x, -z) to (1, 1) at (+x, +z); counter-clockwise seen from above.

| Parameter `z_segments` | cells along Z, at least 1. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the mesh; must not be null. |
| Returns | null on success, otherwise a static message. |
| Fails | an argument out of range. |
| Aborts | `out` is null. |

| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call; the message is static. |

## `MakePlaneStripMesh(f32,f32,u32,u32,MeshData*)` {#renderer-makeplanestripmesh-f32-f32-u32-u32-meshdata-star}

`function renderer::MakePlaneStripMesh` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
const char* MakePlaneStripMesh(f32 width, f32 depth, u32 x_segments, u32 z_segments, MeshData* out);
```

Builds the same grid as [`renderer::MakePlaneMesh`](#renderer-makeplanemesh-f32-f32-u32-u32-meshdata-star) with the same vertices, but as triangle strips: one strip per row of cells, ended by a restart index, so the index count is about two thirds of the list version's. The triangles are the same set with the same winding (counter-clockwise seen from above), so the mesh draws identically; its topology is `TriangleStrip` and it has restart markers, so it needs a pipeline with `primitive_restart`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `width` | size along X, greater than zero. |
| Parameter `depth` | size along Z, greater than zero. |
| Parameter `x_segments` | cells along X, at least 1. |
| Parameter `z_segments` | cells along Z, at least 1. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the mesh; must not be null. |
| Returns | null on success, otherwise a static message. |
| Fails | an argument out of range, or more than 65535 vertices (16-bit strip indices reserve the last value). |
| Aborts | `out` is null. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call; the message is static. |

## `MakeColoredMesh(const MeshData&,const u8*,u32,MeshData*)` {#renderer-makecoloredmesh-const-meshdata-amp-const-u8-star-u32-meshdata-star}

`function renderer::MakeColoredMesh` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
const char* MakeColoredMesh(const MeshData& source, const u8* colors, u32 color_count, MeshData* out);
```

Copies a mesh built with [`renderer::StandardVertexLayout`](#renderer-standardvertexlayout) into one with [`renderer::ColorVertexLayout`](#renderer-colorvertexlayout), giving each vertex its own color.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `source` | a valid mesh whose layout is the standard one. |
| Parameter `colors` | `color_count` RGBA colors, four bytes each (sRGB bytes, see [`renderer::ColorVertex`](#renderer-colorvertex)). |
| Parameter `color_count` | must equal the source's vertex count. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the colored mesh, which keeps the source's indices, topology and bounds; must not be null and not be `source`. |
| Returns | null on success, otherwise a static message. |
| Fails | the source is empty or not in the standard layout, or `color_count` differs from its vertex count. |
| Aborts | `out` is null or is `source`. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | `colors` is borrowed for the call and copied; `out` is borrowed for the call. |

## `StripJoin` {#renderer-stripjoin}

`enum renderer::StripJoin` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
enum class StripJoin : u8 { ... }
```

How [`renderer::ListToStrip`](#renderer-listtostrip-const-meshdata-amp-stripjoin-meshdata-star) joins strips that cannot be continued.

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| <a id="renderer-stripjoin-restart"></a>`Restart` | `0` | Put a restart index between strips; the result needs a pipeline with `primitive_restart`. |

## `StripToList(const MeshData&,MeshData*)` {#renderer-striptolist-const-meshdata-amp-meshdata-star}

`function renderer::StripToList` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
const char* StripToList(const MeshData& strip, MeshData* out);
```

Converts a triangle-strip mesh to a triangle list: the same vertices, one triangle per strip position, restart markers honored, degenerate triangles (two equal indices) dropped, winding alternation undone so every triangle keeps the orientation it is drawn with.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `strip` | a valid mesh with topology `TriangleStrip`. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the list mesh; must not be null and not be `strip`. |
| Returns | null on success, otherwise a static message. |
| Fails | the mesh is empty or not a triangle strip, or the strip holds no triangle. |
| Aborts | `out` is null or is `strip`. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call. |

## `ListToStrip(const MeshData&,StripJoin,MeshData*)` {#renderer-listtostrip-const-meshdata-amp-stripjoin-meshdata-star}

`function renderer::ListToStrip` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
const char* ListToStrip(const MeshData& list, StripJoin join, MeshData* out);
```

Converts a triangle-list mesh to triangle strips by greedy edge walking: each strip is extended through the edge shared with an unused neighbor of the right orientation until none is left, then the next strip starts, joined as `join` says. The set of triangles, and every triangle's winding, is preserved; the number of indices is usually well below the list's (three per triangle) and never above six per triangle (a lone triangle joined by degenerate vertices).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `list` | a valid mesh with topology `TriangleList`. |
| Parameter `join` | how to join the strips. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the strip mesh (topology `TriangleStrip`); must not be null and not be `list`. |
| Returns | null on success, otherwise a static message. |
| Fails | the mesh is empty or not a triangle list. A list with exactly 65536 vertices gets 32-bit strip indices, because 0xFFFF is the restart marker. |
| Aborts | `out` is null or is the source list. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. |
| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call. |

## `GpuMesh` {#renderer-gpumesh}

`class renderer::GpuMesh` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
class GpuMesh final : public Recreatable { ... }
```

A mesh on the GPU: a vertex buffer and an index buffer with the draw information.

`Create` uploads through the device's staging ring and leaves the buffers in the `CopyDst` state; record [`renderer::GpuMesh::RecordPrepare`](#renderer-gpumesh-recordprepare-rhi-commandlist-star) in every command list that draws it until a list containing it has been submitted and [`renderer::GpuMesh::MarkReady`](#renderer-gpumesh-markready) was called, so the buffers move to their read states once.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible. Move-only. |
| Ownership | owns its two buffers; the device must outlive it and be idle (or at least done with the buffers' frames) for a clean exit. Destruction goes through the device's deferred destruction. |

### `CreateFromGeometry(rhi::Device*,const MeshGeometry&,DeviceResources*,const char*)` {#renderer-gpumesh-createfromgeometry-rhi-device-star-const-meshgeometry-amp-deviceresources-star-const-char-star}

`method renderer::GpuMesh::CreateFromGeometry` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
bool CreateFromGeometry(rhi::Device* device, const MeshGeometry& geometry, DeviceResources* resources, const char* debug_name);
```

Creates the buffers from geometry read in place and keeps the geometry (and with it a share of its bytes) as the source for a device reset: the bytes are uploaded from where they are, now and after every reset, and the mesh registers with `resources` (a mesh without a registry does not survive a reset).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the device; must outlive this object. |
| Parameter `geometry` | the geometry; valid ([`renderer::MeshGeometry::Validate`](#renderer-meshgeometry-validate-const)). |
| Parameter `resources` | the registry, or null. |
| Parameter `debug_name` | a label for the buffers; copied, may be null. |
| Returns | true on success; on failure nothing stays allocated and the failure was logged. |
| Fails | invalid geometry, or the device cannot allocate or upload. |
| Aborts | this mesh already holds buffers. |
| Ownership | shares the bytes `geometry` holds; they live as long as this mesh does. |

### `MakeRecreatable(DeviceResources*,const MeshData&,const char*)` {#renderer-gpumesh-makerecreatable-deviceresources-star-const-meshdata-amp-const-char-star}

`method renderer::GpuMesh::MakeRecreatable` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
bool MakeRecreatable(DeviceResources* resources, const MeshData& data, const char* debug_name);
```

Makes this mesh survive a device reset: copies `data` into a shared region kept as the source, and registers with `resources`.

After this, `Release` and `Recreate` work and the registry calls them on every reset. Call after a successful `Create` from the same geometry; a source that no longer matches the mesh fails the reset, naming the mesh.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `resources` | the registry; null records nothing and registers nothing. |
| Parameter `data` | the geometry the mesh was created from. |
| Parameter `debug_name` | a label for the rebuilt buffers; copied, may be null. |
| Returns | true if registered (or `resources` is null). |
| Fails | returns false if the registry refused; the mesh is then still valid but will not survive a reset. |
| Aborts | the mesh is empty, or `data` is empty. |
| Ownership | `data` is read during the call only; the copy is owned by this mesh. |

### `Release()` {#renderer-gpumesh-release}

`method renderer::GpuMesh::Release` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
void Release() override;
```

Destroys the buffers and keeps everything else (the source, the bounds, the sort id). The mesh reads as not `Valid` until `Recreate`. Safe on a released mesh.

### `Recreate(rhi::Device*)` {#renderer-gpumesh-recreate-rhi-device-star}

`method renderer::GpuMesh::Recreate` — defined in `engine/renderer/mesh.h`

```cpp
bool Recreate(rhi::Device* device) override;
```

Uploads the geometry again on `device`; the buffer handles change, the sort id does not.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the new device. |
| Returns | true on success. |
| Fails | returns false, logging the reason, if the mesh has no source, the loader fails, or the upload fails. |
| Aborts | the mesh already holds buffers. |
| Ownership | borrowed for the call; nothing is kept unless the description above says so. |

## `GraphUsage` {#renderer-graphusage}

`enum renderer::GraphUsage` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_graph.h`

```cpp
enum class GraphUsage : u8 { ... }
```

How a pass uses a resource; each maps to one [`rhi::ResourceState`](rhi.md#rhi-resourcestate) (see [`renderer::StateForUsage`](#renderer-stateforusage-graphusage)).

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-colorattachment"></a>`ColorAttachment` | `0` | Rendered to as a color attachment (texture). Write, or ReadWrite when blending or loading. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-depthattachment"></a>`DepthAttachment` |  | Depth(-stencil) attachment with writes (texture). Write, or ReadWrite with a depth test. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-depthread"></a>`DepthRead` |  | Depth read-only: the depth test without writes, or sampled (texture). Read. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-sampled"></a>`Sampled` |  | Sampled by shaders (texture). Read. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-storageread"></a>`StorageRead` |  | Read by shaders as a storage image or buffer. Read. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-storagewrite"></a>`StorageWrite` |  | Written by shaders as a storage image or buffer. Write, or ReadWrite. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-copysrc"></a>`CopySrc` |  | Source of a copy. Read. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-copydst"></a>`CopyDst` |  | Destination of a copy. Write. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-uniformbuffer"></a>`UniformBuffer` |  | Read as a uniform buffer (buffer). Read. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-vertexbuffer"></a>`VertexBuffer` |  | Read as vertex data (buffer). Read. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-indexbuffer"></a>`IndexBuffer` |  | Read as index data (buffer). Read. |
| <a id="renderer-graphusage-count"></a>`Count` |  | Number of usages; not a usage. |

## `RenderGraph` {#renderer-rendergraph}

`class renderer::RenderGraph` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_graph.h`

```cpp
class RenderGraph { ... }
```

A frame's pass and resource declarations and their compiled schedule.

The order of use is: create the resources, `AddPass` and declare accesses, `Compile`, then read the schedule. Names are borrowed and must outlive the graph (string literals are the normal case). `Compile` takes scratch space from the arena.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible. |
| Ownership | borrows the arena, which must outlive the graph and not be reset while the graph is in use; all graph storage lives in it, so the graph has nothing to free. |

### `RenderGraph(ArenaAllocator*,const RenderGraphDesc&)` {#renderer-rendergraph-rendergraph-arenaallocator-star-const-rendergraphdesc-amp}

`constructor renderer::RenderGraph::RenderGraph` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_graph.h`


### `Compile()` {#renderer-rendergraph-compile}

`method renderer::RenderGraph::Compile` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_graph.h`

```cpp
bool Compile();
```

Checks the declarations, orders the passes, drops the unused ones and finds each resource's first and last use.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | true if the graph is valid; false after storing the errors (see `ErrorCount`). Call once per frame: a second call with nothing declared in between returns true at once, and any declaration after a call makes the next one run again (and take more scratch space from the arena). |
| Fails | any error described at the declaring calls, a read of a transient resource no earlier pass writes, a cycle through explicit dependencies, a transient resource used in a way its description's usage flags do not allow (a description with no usage fits no use), or the arena running out. |

### `BarrierCount()` {#renderer-rendergraph-barriercount-const}

`method renderer::RenderGraph::BarrierCount` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_graph.h`

```cpp
u32 BarrierCount() const;
```

Returns the number of barriers the compiled graph records. Valid after a successful `Compile`.

The rules, per resource, walking the scheduled passes in order: a resource starts in its import's initial state, or `Undefined` for a transient one. Each use needs the state of its usage (see [`renderer::StateForUsage`](#renderer-stateforusage-graphusage)). A barrier is recorded when that state differs from the current one, or when the previous use wrote the resource (a same-state barrier then orders the two writes, or the write and the read). Consecutive reads in one state share the state and need nothing. Barriers for all of a pass's resources precede it, in access order, so a pass's color and depth attachments change state together and always outside a rendering pass. After the last pass, an import not in its final state gets an exit barrier. A transient resource has no exit barrier; the pool remembers its state.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | never. |

| Field | Value |
