### `Execute(TransientPool*,rhi::CommandList*)` {#renderer-rendergraph-execute-transientpool-star-rhi-commandlist-star}

`method renderer::RenderGraph::Execute` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_graph.h`

```cpp
bool Execute(TransientPool* pool, rhi::CommandList* list);
```

Records the compiled graph into a command list: transients from the pool, barriers, then each pass's callback.

A transient resource is taken from the pool at its first use and given back after its last, so a later one in the same frame can reuse it. Passes record into `list`, which must be open and outside a rendering pass. The graph transitions every resource itself, so a pass callback records no barrier for a resource it declared. An import is assumed to be in its declared initial state and is left in its final state.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `pool` | where transient resources come from; may be null if the graph declares none. |
| Parameter `list` | the open command list. |
| Returns | true if everything was recorded. |
| Fails | `Compile` fails, a transient resource could not be allocated, or `pool` is null while a transient is used; `ExecuteError` then names the cause. The list is incomplete and must not be submitted; every transient taken goes back to the pool in the state it was taken in, as if the call had not happened. |
| Aborts | `list` is null. `Device::CreateCommandList` returns null when the device is lost, so check it first; a null list is an environment failure for the caller to handle, not something this function reports. |
| Ownership | `pool` and `list` are borrowed for the call; transient resources taken from the pool go back to it before `Execute` returns. |

### `ExecuteParallel(TransientPool*,rhi::Device*,jobs::Scheduler*,rhi::FenceValue*)` {#renderer-rendergraph-executeparallel-transientpool-star-rhi-device-star-jobs-scheduler-star-rhi-fencevalue-star}

`method renderer::RenderGraph::ExecuteParallel` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_graph.h`

```cpp
bool ExecuteParallel(TransientPool* pool, rhi::Device* device, jobs::Scheduler* scheduler, rhi::FenceValue* fence);
```

Records the compiled graph like `Execute`, but one command list per pass, in parallel, and submits them in order.

Transients are resolved first (serially), then every scheduled pass records into its own list: with a scheduler the passes record concurrently on its participants (each list is created on the thread that records it), without one they record in order on the calling thread. A pass with no callback and no barriers records no list at all. A last list holds the exit barriers of the imports. All lists go to one `Submit` in schedule order, so the result is the same as a serial `Execute`: a pass sees the state the plan gives it, whichever thread recorded it.

Pass callbacks run concurrently with each other and must not share mutable state without synchronization; the graph itself is only read while they run.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `pool` | where transient resources come from; may be null if the graph declares none. |
| Parameter `device` | the device; a frame must be open (`BeginFrame`). |
| Parameter `scheduler` | runs the recording, or null to record on the calling thread. Must be called from participant 0 outside any job when set. |
| Parameter `fence` | receives the fence value of the submission; may be null. |
| Returns | true if everything was recorded and submitted. |
| Fails | as for `Execute`, and when the device cannot give out a command list or refuses the submission (lost); `ExecuteError` names the cause. Nothing is submitted then and the pool is put back as it was, but pass callbacks may already have run (some or all of them, and on several threads at once), so any state they changed outside the command list stays changed. |
| Aborts | `device` is null, or no frame is open. |
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only (a scheduler's participant 0). |
| Ownership | `pool`, `device` and `scheduler` are borrowed for the call. |

### `Dump(char*,usize)` {#renderer-rendergraph-dump-char-star-usize-const}

`method renderer::RenderGraph::Dump` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_graph.h`

```cpp
usize Dump(char* out, usize capacity) const;
```

Writes a text description of the graph: passes, accesses, resources and edges.

The format is line oriented. After a header line (`graph passes=P resources=R scheduled=S culled=C`) come `schedule: a, b, c` and `culled: x, y` lines, then one block per pass (`pass I "name" [side-effects] at K` or `culled`, followed by one indented `read|write|readwrite "resource" usage` line per access), one line per resource (`resource I "name" texture|buffer transient|import|import-readonly use=F..L` or `unused`) and one line per edge (`edge "from" -> "to" raw|waw|war|after ["resource"]`). Before a successful `Compile` only the passes and resources are listed.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `out` | the destination, or null to only measure. |
| Parameter `capacity` | size of `out` in bytes. |
| Returns | the length the full text needs, excluding the terminator; the output is truncated (and terminated) if it exceeds `capacity - 1`. |
| Fails | never. |
| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call. |

### `WriteDot(char*,usize)` {#renderer-rendergraph-writedot-char-star-usize-const}

`method renderer::RenderGraph::WriteDot` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_graph.h`


| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `out` | the destination, or null to only measure. |
| Parameter `capacity` | size of `out` in bytes. |
| Returns | the length the full text needs, excluding the terminator; truncated as for `Dump`. |
| Fails | never. |
| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call. |

## `ObjectId` {#renderer-objectid}

`struct renderer::ObjectId` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_scene.h`

```cpp
struct ObjectId { ... }
```

A generation-checked reference to an object in a [`renderer::RenderScene`](#renderer-renderscene), like an RHI handle: the all-zero value is invalid, and an id whose object was removed no longer matches even if its slot is reused.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-safe plain value. |

The slot in the scene.


The slot's generation when the id was made; 0 means invalid.

|---|---|


### `joint_matrices` {#renderer-renderobjectdesc-joint-matrices}

`variable renderer::RenderObjectDesc::joint_matrices` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_scene.h`

```cpp
const f32* joint_matrices;
```

The joint palette of a skinned mesh: `joint_count` row-major 3x4 matrices (12 `f32` each, the animation runtime's `SkeletonInstance::palette`). Null for a rigid mesh. Borrowed: the memory must stay valid, and hold the pose to draw, until the frame that reads it has been prepared; the renderer copies it into the frame's palette buffer then and keeps no pointer to it afterwards.

### `joint_count` {#renderer-renderobjectdesc-joint-count}

`variable renderer::RenderObjectDesc::joint_count` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_scene.h`


### `skin_bounds_scale` {#renderer-renderobjectdesc-skin-bounds-scale}

`variable renderer::RenderObjectDesc::skin_bounds_scale` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_scene.h`

```cpp
f32 skin_bounds_scale;
```

Scales the mesh's bind-pose bounds about their center for culling (1 = as authored). A pose can move vertices outside the bind-pose box; the game sets this above 1 for a clip that does.

## `kMaxLights` {#renderer-kmaxlights}

`variable renderer::kMaxLights` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_scene.h`

```cpp
inline constexpr u32 kMaxLights;
```

The most lights a scene or a [`renderer::FrameResources`](#renderer-frameresources) can hold: 16384.

A light is 48 bytes in the light buffer, so that is 768 KiB per frame slot. Asking for more aborts; a frame with more visible lights than the buffer holds reports the excess in [`renderer::LightSummary::dropped`](#renderer-lightsummary-dropped) and logs an error.

## `RenderScene` {#renderer-renderscene}

`class renderer::RenderScene` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_scene.h`

```cpp
class RenderScene { ... }
```

A fixed-capacity list of objects and lights.

Storage is allocated once at construction, so adding and removing never allocates or moves anything and slot numbers stay valid for the life of an object. Objects are found by [`renderer::ObjectId`](#renderer-objectid); the frame iterates slots (see [`renderer::RenderScene::SlotCount`](#renderer-renderscene-slotcount-const)) and skips the dead ones.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible; reading from several threads at once is fine while nobody writes. |
| Ownership | owns its storage; the meshes the objects point at are borrowed and must outlive them. |

### `RenderScene(const RenderSceneDesc&)` {#renderer-renderscene-renderscene-const-renderscenedesc-amp}

```cpp
explicit RenderScene(const RenderSceneDesc& desc);
```

Allocates the storage.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `desc` | capacities; both at least 1. |
| Aborts | a capacity is 0. |

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|

| <a id="renderer-renderscene-cullmode-flat"></a>`Flat` |  | A scan of every slot: the reference, kept to compare the two paths. |

## `CostClass` {#renderer-costclass}

`enum renderer::CostClass` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_settings.h`

```cpp
enum class CostClass : u8 { ... }
```

What changing a setting costs. Ordered: a larger class includes the actions of every smaller one.

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| <a id="renderer-costclass-none"></a>`None` | `0` | Takes effect without touching the GPU or any frame. |
| <a id="renderer-costclass-dynamic"></a>`Dynamic` |  | Changes a parameter that the next frame reads. |
| <a id="renderer-costclass-swapchain"></a>`Swapchain` |  | Recreates the swapchain and what depends on its size or format. |
| <a id="renderer-costclass-pipelinerebuild"></a>`PipelineRebuild` |  | Releases and recreates pipelines, material bind groups and passes; the device stays. |
| <a id="renderer-costclass-devicereset"></a>`DeviceReset` |  | Destroys and creates the device; every device-dependent object is rebuilt. |

## `CostClassName(CostClass)` {#renderer-costclassname-costclass}

`function renderer::CostClassName` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_settings.h`

```cpp
const char* CostClassName(CostClass cost);
```

Returns a cost class's name (`none`, `dynamic`, `swapchain`, `pipeline-rebuild`, `device-reset`).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `cost` | any class. |
| Returns | a static string. |
| Fails | never. |
| Ownership | the string is static and never freed. |

## `SettingsRegistry` {#renderer-settingsregistry}

`class renderer::SettingsRegistry` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_settings.h`

```cpp
class SettingsRegistry { ... }
```

The settings that exist, with their types, defaults, ranges and costs.

A registry is filled at start-up and then only read. [`renderer::SettingsRegistry::Builtin`](#renderer-settingsregistry-builtin) is the process-wide one that the renderer uses; a test builds its own with [`renderer::RegisterBuiltinSettings`](#renderer-registerbuiltinsettings-settingsregistry-star) plus whatever it wants to add.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible. Register only before any thread reads the registry. |
| Ownership | borrows the strings in each [`renderer::SettingDesc`](#renderer-settingdesc). |

### `Register(const SettingDesc&)` {#renderer-settingsregistry-register-const-settingdesc-amp}

`method renderer::SettingsRegistry::Register` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_settings.h`

```cpp
SettingId Register(const SettingDesc& desc);
```

Registers a setting.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `desc` | the declaration; copied (the strings are borrowed). |
| Returns | the new id, or an invalid id after logging the reason. |
| Fails | the registry is full, the name is empty, too long, uses other characters or is already taken, an `Enum` has no or too many names or a default outside them, an `Int` or `Float` has `min` above `max` or a default outside its range, a `Float` bound is not finite, a `Text` default is too long or has edge blanks, an `Enum` name is empty, null, longer than 31 characters or has a blank, `=` or `#`. |

### `Count()` {#renderer-settingsregistry-count-const}

`method renderer::SettingsRegistry::Count` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_settings.h`

```cpp

```

Returns the number of registered settings.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Fails | never. |

```cpp

```

Returns a setting's declaration.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `id` | any id. |
| Returns | the declaration, or null if `id` is not registered. |
| Fails | never. |
| Ownership | borrowed; valid for the registry's lifetime. |

### `FindByName(const char*)` {#renderer-settingsregistry-findbyname-const-char-star-const}

## `SetStatus` {#renderer-setstatus}

`enum renderer::SetStatus` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_settings.h`

```cpp
enum class SetStatus : u8 { ... }
```

What a call that sets a value reports.

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| <a id="renderer-setstatus-ok"></a>`Ok` | `0` | The value was stored as given. |
| <a id="renderer-setstatus-clamped"></a>`Clamped` |  | The value was stored after moving it into its range. |
| <a id="renderer-setstatus-outofrange"></a>`OutOfRange` |  | The value is outside its range or not finite, and the setting is unchanged. |
| <a id="renderer-setstatus-wrongtype"></a>`WrongType` |  | The call does not match the setting's type; unchanged. |
| <a id="renderer-setstatus-unknownid"></a>`UnknownId` |  | No such setting; unchanged. |
| <a id="renderer-setstatus-badtext"></a>`BadText` |  | The text is too long or has characters outside printable ASCII; unchanged. |

`alias renderer::SettingsKeyMigrationFn` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_settings.h`

```cpp
using SettingsKeyMigrationFn = const char* (*)(u32 file_version, const char* key);
```

Renames a key written by an older format version.

Called with the version the text declared and the key found; returns the current key to use instead, or null to keep the key.

## `ParseSettings(const char*,usize,RenderSettings*,SettingsParseReport*,SettingsKeyMigrationFn)` {#renderer-parsesettings-const-char-star-usize-rendersettings-star-settingsparsereport-star-settingskeymigrationfn}

`function renderer::ParseSettings` — defined in `engine/renderer/render_settings.h`

```cpp
bool ParseSettings(const char* text, usize length, RenderSettings* settings, SettingsParseReport* report, SettingsKeyMigrationFn migrate = nullptr);
```

Reads settings from text onto `settings`.

Lines are `key = value`; blank lines and lines starting with `#` are skipped; `version = N` declares the format. Keys that are not registered are warnings and skipped. A malformed line, a bad value or a repeated key is an error. When there is any error `settings` is left unchanged, so a half-read file is never applied.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `text` | the text. |
| Parameter `length` | its length in bytes. |
| Parameter `settings` | the settings to change; keys the text omits keep their value. |
| Parameter `report` | receives the findings; may be null. |
| Parameter `migrate` | renames keys of older versions, or null. |
| Returns | true if there were no errors. |
| Fails | returns false and records the line and reason in `report`; a version newer than [`renderer::kSettingsFormatVersion`](#renderer-ksettingsformatversion) is an error. |
| Ownership | `text`, `settings`, `report` and `migrate` are borrowed for the call. |

## `FrameStatus` {#renderer-framestatus}

`enum renderer::FrameStatus` — defined in `engine/renderer/renderer.h`

```cpp
enum class FrameStatus : u8 { ... }
```

How a frame ended.

| Enumerator | Value | Description |
|---|---|---|
| <a id="renderer-framestatus-ok"></a>`Ok` | `0` | The frame was recorded, submitted and (with a swapchain) presented. |
| <a id="renderer-framestatus-skipped"></a>`Skipped` |  | Nothing to draw to (the window is minimized); no work was submitted. Try again next frame. |
| <a id="renderer-framestatus-devicelost"></a>`DeviceLost` |  | The device or the swapchain surface is lost. The renderer stays destroyable but cannot render again. |
| <a id="renderer-framestatus-failed"></a>`Failed` |  | The frame could not be built or recorded (a bad graph, an allocation failure); see `LastError`. Later frames may succeed. |


What the renderer does when the device (or its swapchain surface) is lost.

Needs a [`renderer::RendererDesc::host`](#renderer-rendererdesc-host); without one a loss stays terminal. Attempts and delays are counted in frames, not seconds, so tests are deterministic.

| Thread-safety | thread-compatible plain data. |

`variable renderer::RecoveryPolicy::max_attempts` — defined in `engine/renderer/renderer.h`

```cpp

```

The most reset attempts for one loss; 0 turns recovery off (the loss is terminal, as before Phase 8).

### `delay_frames` {#renderer-recoverypolicy-delay-frames}

`variable renderer::RecoveryPolicy::delay_frames` — defined in `engine/renderer/renderer.h`

```cpp
u32 delay_frames;
```

Frames to wait before the second attempt; each later attempt waits twice as long as the one before, up to `max_delay_frames`. The first attempt is made at once, in the frame that found the loss.

### `max_delay_frames` {#renderer-recoverypolicy-max-delay-frames}

`variable renderer::RecoveryPolicy::max_delay_frames` — defined in `engine/renderer/renderer.h`

```cpp

```

The longest wait between attempts, in frames.

```cpp

```

Whether the second and later attempts may use the best adapter instead of the saved one when the saved one keeps failing.

### `Rebind(rhi::Device*,rhi::Swapchain*)` {#renderer-renderer-rebind-rhi-device-star-rhi-swapchain-star}

`method renderer::Renderer::Rebind` — defined in `engine/renderer/renderer.h`

```cpp
void Rebind(rhi::Device* device, rhi::Swapchain* swapchain);
```

Points the renderer at a new device and swapchain after the device was replaced. The renderer keeps no other device handle between frames: its transient pool is rebuilt through the registry, and the per-frame state is rebuilt by the next `RenderFrame`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the new device. |
| Parameter `swapchain` | the new swapchain, or null for offscreen rendering. |
| Aborts | `device` is null, or the renderer was built without `RendererDesc::resources` (its pool could not follow the device). |
| Ownership | both are borrowed. |

### `ApplySettings(const RenderSettings&)` {#renderer-renderer-applysettings-const-rendersettings-amp}

`method renderer::Renderer::ApplySettings` — defined in `engine/renderer/renderer.h`

```cpp
ApplyResult ApplySettings(const RenderSettings& requested);
```

Changes the rendering settings at run time, doing the cheapest sufficient thing.

The change is diffed against the settings in force ([`renderer::DiffSettings`](#renderer-diffsettings-const-rendersettings-amp-const-rendersettings-amp-const-capabilities-amp)) and its highest cost class is performed: `None` and `Dynamic` store the values; `Swapchain` reconfigures the swapchain (and asks the window for fullscreen); `PipelineRebuild` releases and recreates the registry's tier 3 and above (materials, passes); `DeviceReset` replaces the device through the host and every registered owner rebuilds. If a step fails the previous settings are restored; if that fails too the renderer is device-lost. Settings that need a missing capability (fullscreen, HDR) are left as they were.

Called inside a frame (from the build callback, which runs on the owning thread) the request is queued and applied at the start of the next `RenderFrame`; a newer request replaces a pending one, also one made later outside a frame. Call from the owning thread only (never from a job); workers that record must be idle.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `requested` | the settings wanted. |
| Returns | what happened; `queued` if it waits for the frame boundary. |
| Fails | `ok` is false with `reason` set; `rolled_back` or `device_lost` say what state the renderer is in. |

### `ForceDeviceReset()` {#renderer-renderer-forcedevicereset}

`method renderer::Renderer::ForceDeviceReset` — defined in `engine/renderer/renderer.h`

```cpp
ApplyResult ForceDeviceReset();
```

Replaces the device with one made from the settings in force, although nothing changed (a reset on request).

Same sequence, thread rule and failure handling as a device-reset change in `ApplySettings`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | what happened; `performed` is `DeviceReset` on success. |
| Fails | as for `ApplySettings`. |

### `RenderFrame(const RenderScene*,const Camera*,const FrameTarget*)` {#renderer-renderer-renderframe-const-renderscene-star-const-camera-star-const-frametarget-star}

`method renderer::Renderer::RenderFrame` — defined in `engine/renderer/renderer.h`

```cpp
FrameStatus RenderFrame(const RenderScene* scene, const Camera* camera, const FrameTarget* offscreen);
```

Renders one frame.

Opens a device frame, acquires the swapchain image (or imports `offscreen`), builds the graph through the callback, compiles and records it, submits once, closes the frame and presents. With a swapchain, every path that acquired an image also presents it, so a failed frame does not leave the swapchain stuck.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `scene` | passed to the callback; may be null. |
| Parameter `camera` | passed to the callback; may be null. |
| Parameter `offscreen` | the target when there is no swapchain; ignored with one. |
| Returns | how the frame ended. |
| Fails | `Skipped` when the swapchain is minimized; `DeviceLost` when the device or surface is lost; `Failed` for anything else (the reason is in `LastError`). |
| Aborts | there is no swapchain and `offscreen` is null or has an invalid texture. |

## `RenderLogV(LogVerbosity,const char*,...)` {#renderer-renderlogv-logverbosity-const-char-star}

`function renderer::RenderLogV` — defined in `engine/renderer/renderer_log.h`

```cpp
void RenderLogV(LogVerbosity level, const char* fmt, ...) ENGINE_PRINTF_FORMAT(2, 3);
```

Logs a printf-style message on the foundation's `LogRender` channel using a 512-byte stack buffer and no allocator.

The renderer reports environment problems (a format the device cannot render to, a failed pipeline), never per-draw events. It shares `LogRender` with the rest of the engine, so `--log-level` applies. Output longer than the buffer is truncated, and nothing is formatted when the level is filtered out.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `level` | verbosity of the message. |
| Parameter `fmt` | printf format string; the arguments follow it. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. Formats into a stack buffer and logs through the shared sink. |

## `ValidateShaderPermutations(const ShaderPermutations&)` {#renderer-validateshaderpermutations-const-shaderpermutations-amp}

`function renderer::ValidateShaderPermutations` — defined in `engine/renderer/shader_permutation.h`

```cpp
const char* ValidateShaderPermutations(const ShaderPermutations& table);
```

Checks that a table is internally consistent.

Option names are non-empty and unique, counts are within the limits, entries are sorted and unique, keys use only declared options, module indices are in range, the base key is present and uses module 0, and every module is non-empty.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `table` | the table to check. |
| Returns | null if it is consistent, else a static string naming the first problem. |
| Fails | reported through the return value, never by aborting. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its argument. |
| Ownership | the returned string is static and is never freed by the caller. |

## `MakePermutationKey(const ShaderPermutations&,const char* const*,u32,PermutationKey*,ReflectionError*)` {#renderer-makepermutationkey-const-shaderpermutations-amp-const-char-star-const-star-u32-permutationkey-star-reflectionerror-star}

`function renderer::MakePermutationKey` — defined in `engine/renderer/shader_permutation.h`

```cpp
bool MakePermutationKey(const ShaderPermutations& table, const char* const* names, u32 count, PermutationKey* out, ReflectionError* error);
```

Builds a key from option names.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `table` | the permuted shader. |
| Parameter `names` | pointers to nul-terminated option names; may be null if `count` is 0. |
| Parameter `count` | number of names. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the key; must not be null. |
| Parameter `error` | receives a message naming the shader and the unknown option on failure; may be null. |
| Returns | true if every name is a declared option. |
| Fails | reported through the return value and `error`. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |
| Ownership | all pointers are borrowed for the call. |

## `FindPermutation(const ShaderPermutations&,PermutationKey,ReflectionError*)` {#renderer-findpermutation-const-shaderpermutations-amp-permutationkey-reflectionerror-star}

`function renderer::FindPermutation` — defined in `engine/renderer/shader_permutation.h`

```cpp
const PermutationModule* FindPermutation(const ShaderPermutations& table, PermutationKey key, ReflectionError* error);
```

Finds the module built for a key. The search is linear over at most [`renderer::kMaxPermutationKeys`](#renderer-kmaxpermutationkeys) entries.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `table` | the permuted shader. |
| Parameter `key` | the options that are on. |
| Parameter `error` | receives a message naming the shader and the key (for example `shader 'x.frag' has no permutation {INVERT, HALF}`) on failure; may be null. |
| Returns | a pointer into `table`, or null if the key was not built (or the table is corrupt, which the message says). |
| Fails | reported through the return value and `error`; nothing is substituted. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |
| Ownership | the pointer refers to static data and stays valid as long as `table`'s storage does. |

## `DescribePermutationKey(const ShaderPermutations&,PermutationKey,char*,usize)` {#renderer-describepermutationkey-const-shaderpermutations-amp-permutationkey-char-star-usize}

`function renderer::DescribePermutationKey` — defined in `engine/renderer/shader_permutation.h`

```cpp
void DescribePermutationKey(const ShaderPermutations& table, PermutationKey key, char* out, usize capacity);
```

Writes a key as `{A, B}` (or `{}` for the base key).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `table` | the permuted shader, for the option names. |
| Parameter `key` | the key to describe. |
| Parameter `out` | receives a nul-terminated string, truncated to fit. |
| Parameter `capacity` | size of `out` in bytes; 0 writes nothing. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |
| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call. |

## `ValidateShaderReflection(const ShaderReflection&)` {#renderer-validateshaderreflection-const-shaderreflection-amp}

`function renderer::ValidateShaderReflection` — defined in `engine/renderer/shader_reflection.h`

```cpp
const char* ValidateShaderReflection(const ShaderReflection& reflection);
```

Checks that a reflection is internally consistent: known enum values, terminated names, counts within capacity, block indices in range, a stage that matches the compute local size.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `reflection` | the reflection to check. |
| Returns | null if it is consistent, else a static string naming the first problem. |
| Fails | reported through the return value, never by aborting. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its argument. |
| Ownership | the returned string is static and is never freed by the caller. |

## `SerializeShaderReflection(const ShaderReflection&,u8*,usize)` {#renderer-serializeshaderreflection-const-shaderreflection-amp-u8-star-usize}

`function renderer::SerializeShaderReflection` — defined in `engine/renderer/shader_reflection.h`

```cpp
usize SerializeShaderReflection(const ShaderReflection& reflection, u8* out, usize capacity);
```

Encodes a reflection as the little-endian blob stored in `*.refl.inc`.

Call it with `out` null to learn the size needed.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `reflection` | a reflection that passes [`renderer::ValidateShaderReflection`](#renderer-validateshaderreflection-const-shaderreflection-amp). |
| Parameter `out` | where to write the blob, or null to only measure. |
| Parameter `capacity` | size of `out` in bytes. |
| Returns | the size of the blob in bytes; 0 if the reflection is invalid or `out` is non-null and too small. |
| Fails | reported through the return value 0, never by aborting. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |
| Ownership | `out` is borrowed for the call. |

## `ParseShaderReflection(const u8*,usize,ShaderReflection*)` {#renderer-parseshaderreflection-const-u8-star-usize-shaderreflection-star}

`function renderer::ParseShaderReflection` — defined in `engine/renderer/shader_reflection.h`

```cpp
const char* ParseShaderReflection(const u8* data, usize size, ShaderReflection* out);
```

Decodes a reflection blob, rejecting anything malformed without reading outside `data`.

It refuses a wrong magic number or version, truncated or over-long data, names without room for a terminator, counts above the fixed capacities, enum values it does not know, indices out of range and trailing bytes. On failure `out` is left in an unspecified but valid state.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `data` | the blob bytes. |
| Parameter `size` | number of bytes in `data`. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the reflection; must not be null. |
| Returns | null on success, else a static string naming the first problem. |
| Fails | reported through the return value, never by aborting. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |
| Ownership | `data` is borrowed for the call; nothing is retained. |

## `FindBlock(const ShaderReflection&,const char*)` {#renderer-findblock-const-shaderreflection-amp-const-char-star}

| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |
| Ownership | the pointer is valid as long as `reflection` is. |

## `BuildBindGroupLayout(const ShaderReflection* const*,u32,u32,BindGroupLayoutData*,ReflectionError*)` {#renderer-buildbindgrouplayout-const-shaderreflection-star-const-star-u32-u32-bindgrouplayoutdata-star-reflectionerror-star}

`function renderer::BuildBindGroupLayout` — defined in `engine/renderer/shader_reflection.h`

```cpp
bool BuildBindGroupLayout(const ShaderReflection* const* shaders, u32 shader_count, u32 set, BindGroupLayoutData* out, ReflectionError* error);
```

Builds the layout of descriptor set `set` from one or more shader modules, merging a vertex and a fragment shader into one layout.

A binding used by several modules appears once with the stage masks OR-ed. The same (set, binding) with different types or array sizes in two modules is reported with both variable names.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `shaders` | pointers to the reflections to merge. |
| Parameter `shader_count` | number of pointers in `shaders`; at least 1. |
| Parameter `set` | the descriptor set to build. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the layout; must not be null. |
| Parameter `error` | receives a message on failure; may be null. |
| Returns | true on success; false on a conflict or if the set holds more than [`rhi::kMaxBindingsPerLayout`](rhi.md#rhi-kmaxbindingsperlayout) bindings. |
| Fails | reported through the return value and `error`. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |
| Ownership | all pointers are borrowed for the call. |

## `BuildBindGroupLayoutFor(const ShaderReflection&,u32,BindGroupLayoutData*,ReflectionError*)` {#renderer-buildbindgrouplayoutfor-const-shaderreflection-amp-u32-bindgrouplayoutdata-star-reflectionerror-star}

## `BuildVertexLayout(const ShaderReflection&,u32,VertexLayoutData*,ReflectionError*)` {#renderer-buildvertexlayout-const-shaderreflection-amp-u32-vertexlayoutdata-star-reflectionerror-star}

`function renderer::BuildVertexLayout` — defined in `engine/renderer/shader_reflection.h`

```cpp
bool BuildVertexLayout(const ShaderReflection& shader, u32 binding, VertexLayoutData* out, ReflectionError* error);
```

Builds a tightly packed, interleaved vertex layout for the inputs of a vertex shader.

Attributes go in increasing location order, each at the end of the previous one, all in vertex binding `binding`. A mesh whose vertices are laid out differently builds its attributes by hand.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `shader` | a vertex shader's reflection. |
| Parameter `binding` | the vertex buffer binding all attributes read from. |
| Parameter `out` | receives the layout; must not be null. |
| Parameter `error` | receives a message on failure; may be null. |
| Returns | true on success; false if `shader` is not a vertex shader or has more inputs than the RHI allows. |
| Fails | reported through the return value and `error`. |
| Thread-safety | thread-safe. A pure function of its arguments. |
| Ownership | all pointers are borrowed for the call. |

## `ApplySharedChange(void*,const::engine_placeholder::settings::ChangeEvent&)` {#renderer-applysharedchange-void-star-const-engine-placeholder-settings-changeevent-amp}

`function renderer::ApplySharedChange` — defined in `engine/renderer/shared_settings.h`

```cpp
bool ApplySharedChange(void* renderer, const ::engine_placeholder::settings::ChangeEvent& event);
```

The apply function for the `renderer.*` settings: it builds the requested [`renderer::RenderSettings`](#renderer-rendersettings) from the renderer's current ones plus the changes and calls [`renderer::Renderer::ApplySettings`](#renderer-renderer-applysettings-const-rendersettings-amp). Pass it to [`settings::Settings::Apply`](foundation-settings.md#settings-settings-apply-const-changerequest-star-usize-applyfn-void-star) with the renderer as `user`.

A change made while the renderer is inside a frame is queued by the renderer and applied at the next frame; the change is accepted here and can no longer be rolled back through the settings system.

The requested settings are the values the settings system holds (including changes it accepted earlier that the renderer has queued) plus this batch, so a second change made before the first was applied does not replace it.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | false if the renderer could not apply the change (it restored its previous settings), so the settings system rolls the batch back. |
| Fails | reports the renderer's refusal as false. |
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only, as [`renderer::Renderer::ApplySettings`](#renderer-renderer-applysettings-const-rendersettings-amp). |
| Ownership | borrows the renderer for the call. |

## `SyncSharedFromRenderer(Renderer*,::engine_placeholder::settings::Settings*)` {#renderer-syncsharedfromrenderer-renderer-star-engine-placeholder-settings-settings-star}

`function renderer::SyncSharedFromRenderer` — defined in `engine/renderer/shared_settings.h`

```cpp
bool SyncSharedFromRenderer(Renderer* renderer, ::engine_placeholder::settings::Settings* settings);
```

Brings the settings system back in line with the renderer after a queued change failed at the frame boundary: if the renderer's settings in force differ from the values the settings system holds, the renderer's become the settings system's (no apply function runs, listeners are told). Call it after `RenderFrame` in an application that changes settings inside frames.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | whether a correction was needed. |
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | borrows both for the call. |

## `TextRenderer` {#renderer-textrenderer}

`class renderer::TextRenderer` — defined in `engine/renderer/text_renderer.h`

```cpp
class TextRenderer final : public Recreatable { ... }
```

Draws text from a [`text::GlyphAtlas`](text.md#text-glyphatlas): add laid-out text each frame, then record it.

Usage per frame: [`renderer::TextRenderer::BeginFrame`](#renderer-textrenderer-beginframe), any number of [`renderer::TextRenderer::Add`](#renderer-textrenderer-add-const-text-textlayout-amp-const-text-textstyle-amp-f32-f32), then (between `BeginRendering` and `EndRendering` of a pass that writes the target) [`renderer::TextRenderer::Record`](#renderer-textrenderer-record-rhi-commandlist-star-u32-u32). Positions are pixels of the target with the origin at its top-left corner, X right and Y down, as the layout gives them. Glyph quads land on whole pixels, so the atlas bitmaps are shown texel for texel. The result is premultiplied alpha, blended over what is there.

It owns a [`renderer::GlyphAtlasTexture`](#renderer-glyphatlastexture) (the GPU pages), a pipeline, a sampler and per-frame vertex memory, and registers with a [`renderer::DeviceResources`](#renderer-deviceresources); after a reset `Recreate` makes them again and the CPU-side quads of the frame in progress are kept. Only SPIR-V is minted for the text shaders so far, so a Direct3D 12 device refuses `Create` with a logged error (`KNOWN-ISSUES.md`).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only, and not safe to use while another thread adds glyphs to the atlas. |
| Ownership | borrows the atlas (and the fonts the styles name, for the duration of `Add`); owns its GPU objects. The device and the registry must outlive it. |

```cpp
bool Create(rhi::Device* device, text::GlyphAtlas* atlas, const TextRendererDesc& desc, DeviceResources* resources);
```

### `BeginFrame()` {#renderer-textrenderer-beginframe}

`method renderer::TextRenderer::BeginFrame` — defined in `engine/renderer/text_renderer.h`


Forgets the quads of the previous frame. Call it once per frame before `Add`.

### `Add(const text::TextLayout&,const text::TextStyle&,f32,f32)` {#renderer-textrenderer-add-const-text-textlayout-amp-const-text-textstyle-amp-f32-f32}

`method renderer::TextRenderer::Add` — defined in `engine/renderer/text_renderer.h`

```cpp
bool Add(const text::TextLayout& layout, const text::TextStyle& style, f32 x, f32 y);
```

Adds the glyphs of a layout to this frame.

Each glyph is looked up in the atlas (and rasterized into it on first use) at the style's font and size. Glyphs without pixels (spaces) are skipped.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `layout` | the layout of the text, made with `style`'s font and size. |
| Parameter `style` | the font, size and color (packed RGBA, red in the lowest byte); the font's pixel size is set to the style's. |
| Parameter `x` | target pixel the layout's left edge (x = 0) lands on. |
| Parameter `y` | target pixel the layout's top edge (the first line's top) lands on. |
| Returns | true when every glyph was added. |
| Fails | returns false, logging once per frame, when the frame's glyph budget or the atlas ran out; the glyphs that did not fit are counted in [`renderer::TextRenderer::DroppedGlyphs`](#renderer-textrenderer-droppedglyphs-const) and the rest are kept. |
| Aborts | the style has no open font. |
| Ownership | nothing of `layout` or `style` is retained. |

### `Record(rhi::CommandList*,u32,u32)` {#renderer-textrenderer-record-rhi-commandlist-star-u32-u32}

`method renderer::TextRenderer::Record` — defined in `engine/renderer/text_renderer.h`

```cpp
bool Record(rhi::CommandList* list, u32 target_width, u32 target_height);
```

Sends changed atlas pages, writes this frame's quads and records the draws.

Call it inside a rendering pass whose only color attachment has the format given at creation. Does nothing when no glyph was added.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `list` | the command list that is recording the pass. |
| Parameter `target_width` | the pass's width in pixels. |
| Parameter `target_height` | the pass's height in pixels. |
| Returns | true when everything was recorded. |
| Fails | returns false, logging the reason, if an atlas page cannot be sent (nothing is drawn) or a bind group cannot be made; whatever was recorded stays valid. |
| Aborts | the object does not hold its GPU objects, the call is outside a frame, or it is the second call in one frame (the frame's vertex region is written once). |
| Ownership | borrows the list for the call. |

### `Draw(StringView,const text::TextStyle&,const text::LayoutOptions&,f32,f32)` {#renderer-textrenderer-draw-stringview-const-text-textstyle-amp-const-text-layoutoptions-amp-f32-f32}


Lays out `utf8` and adds it: the convenience call a program makes each frame.

The layout is kept in the renderer and reused, so a steady frame allocates nothing per glyph.

|---|---|

| Parameter `style` | font, size and color. |

| Aborts | the style has no open font. |
| Ownership | nothing is retained. |

### `AddToGraph(RenderGraph*,GraphResource,rhi::Format,u32,u32)` {#renderer-textrenderer-addtograph-rendergraph-star-graphresource-rhi-format-u32-u32}

`method renderer::TextRenderer::AddToGraph` — defined in `engine/renderer/text_renderer.h`

```cpp
void AddToGraph(RenderGraph* graph, GraphResource target, rhi::Format format, u32 width, u32 height);
```

Declares the text pass in a frame's graph, after the passes that wrote `target`.

Declares nothing (no pass, no barrier, no cost) when no glyph was added this frame. Otherwise the pass blends the quads into `target` without clearing it. A format different from the current one rebuilds the pipeline here, on the frame's thread.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `graph` | the frame's graph. |
| Parameter `target` | the display target, already written by an earlier pass (the tonemap); accessed as a color attachment, read and written. |
| Parameter `format` | the target's format. |
| Parameter `width` | the target's width in pixels. |
| Parameter `height` | the target's height in pixels. |
| Fails | the pass logs and draws nothing if the pipeline for `format` cannot be made. |
| Aborts | `graph` is null, `format` is undefined, a size is 0, or the object holds no GPU objects. |
| Ownership | the pass keeps a pointer to this object until the graph is executed. |

## `GpuTexture` {#renderer-gputexture}

`class renderer::GpuTexture` — defined in `engine/renderer/texture.h`

```cpp
class GpuTexture final : public Recreatable { ... }
```

A sampled texture that survives a device reset.

It holds either its pixels (`Create`) or a loader (`CreateFromLoader`), registers with a [`renderer::DeviceResources`](#renderer-deviceresources) at the texture tier, and rebuilds itself in `Recreate`. The handle changes after a reset: read `Handle()` each frame, never keep it.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. Move-only. |
| Ownership | owns the texture and the pixels. The device and the registry must outlive it. |

### `Create(rhi::Device*,TextureImage&&,DeviceResources*,const char*)` {#renderer-gputexture-create-rhi-device-star-textureimage-amp-amp-deviceresources-star-const-char-star}

`method renderer::GpuTexture::Create` — defined in `engine/renderer/texture.h`

```cpp
bool Create(rhi::Device* device, TextureImage&& image, DeviceResources* resources, const char* debug_name);
```

Creates the texture from `image` and keeps `image` as the source.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the device. |
| Parameter `image` | the description and pixels; moved in. |
| Parameter `resources` | the registry, or null for a texture that does not need to survive a reset (the pixels are then not kept). |
| Parameter `debug_name` | a label; copied, may be null. |
| Returns | true on success. |
| Fails | returns false, logging the reason, if the description is invalid, exceeds the device limits, the format is not supported, or the upload fails. |
| Aborts | the texture already exists. |
| Ownership | borrowed for the call; nothing is kept unless the description above says so. |

### `CreateFromSharedLevels(rhi::Device*,const rhi::TextureDesc&,SharedBytesPtr,Span<const TextureLevelSpan>,DeviceResources*,const char*)` {#renderer-gputexture-createfromsharedlevels-rhi-device-star-const-rhi-texturedesc-amp-sharedbytesptr-span-lt-const-texturelevelspan-gt-deviceresources-star-const-char-star}

`method renderer::GpuTexture::CreateFromSharedLevels` — defined in `engine/renderer/texture.h`

```cpp
bool CreateFromSharedLevels(rhi::Device* device, const rhi::TextureDesc& desc, SharedBytesPtr bytes, Span<const TextureLevelSpan> levels, DeviceResources* resources, const char* debug_name);
```

Creates the texture from every level of every layer, uploaded straight from a region of shared bytes (the mip sections of an asset blob): the staging copy into device memory is the only copy, and no mip is generated. The texture keeps a share of the region and the offsets of the levels, no pointer into it, so a reset uploads from the same bytes for as long as the texture lives, whoever else lets go of them.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the device. |
| Parameter `desc` | the finished texture; `usage` gets `CopyDst` added; `debug_name` is ignored. |
| Parameter `bytes` | the region the levels are in. |
| Parameter `levels` | exactly one entry for each of `desc.mip_levels * desc.array_layers` subresources. |
| Parameter `resources` | the registry, or null for a texture that does not need to survive a reset. |
| Parameter `debug_name` | a label; copied, may be null. |
| Returns | true on success. |
| Fails | returns false, logging the reason, for no region, an invalid description, a missing, repeated or out-of-range level, a level that is not inside the region, a size or format the device cannot hold, or a failed upload. |
| Aborts | the texture already exists. |
| Ownership | shares `bytes`; `levels` is read during the call and copied. |

### `CreateFromLoader(rhi::Device*,TextureLoader,void*,DeviceResources*,const char*)` {#renderer-gputexture-createfromloader-rhi-device-star-textureloader-void-star-deviceresources-star-const-char-star}

```cpp
bool CreateFromLoader(rhi::Device* device, TextureLoader loader, void* user, DeviceResources* resources, const char* debug_name);
```

Creates the texture from `loader` and keeps only the loader; the pixels are read again at every recreate.

|---|---|

| Parameter `loader` | produces the image. |

| Parameter `resources` | the registry, or null. |
| Parameter `debug_name` | a label; copied, may be null. |
| Returns | true on success. |
| Fails | as for [`renderer::GpuTexture::Create`](#renderer-gputexture-create-rhi-device-star-textureimage-amp-amp-deviceresources-star-const-char-star), and when the loader fails. |
| Aborts | the texture already exists. |
| Ownership | borrowed for the call; nothing is kept unless the description above says so. |

## `TransientPool` {#renderer-transientpool}

`class renderer::TransientPool` — defined in `engine/renderer/transient_pool.h`

```cpp
class TransientPool final : public Recreatable { ... }
```

Pools transient textures and buffers by description.

Two descriptions are equal when every field but the debug name matches. Use per frame is `BeginFrame`, any number of acquire and release calls (the render graph makes them), `EndFrame`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | thread-compatible. |
| Ownership | borrows the device, which must outlive the pool; owns the resources it created and destroys them through the device (so the GPU may still be using them) when it is destroyed or they age out. |


Creates an empty pool.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the device; must not be null. |
| Parameter `desc` | capacities, each at least 1. |
| Aborts | `device` is null or a capacity is 0. |
| Ownership | `device` is borrowed. |

## `UiRenderer` {#renderer-uirenderer}

`class renderer::UiRenderer` — defined in `engine/renderer/ui_renderer.h`

```cpp
class UiRenderer final : public Recreatable { ... }
```

Draws a draw list: the renderer half of the UI toolkit.

Per frame: build a [`ui::DrawList`](ui.md#ui-drawlist) (a [`ui::DrawListCanvas`](ui.md#ui-drawlistcanvas)), call [`ui::DrawList::SortLayers`](ui.md#ui-drawlist-sortlayers), then [`renderer::UiRenderer::AddToGraph`](#renderer-uirenderer-addtograph-rendergraph-star-const-ui-drawlist-star-graphresource-rhi-format-u32-u32-span-lt-const-graphresource-gt) after the passes that wrote the target. The pass loads the target (no clear) and blends premultiplied colour over it. One shader draws every primitive; the mode in each vertex says what it is (see `shaders/ui.frag.slang`). Glyph pages come from a [`renderer::GlyphAtlasTexture`](#renderer-glyphatlastexture) the caller owns (the pass brings its pages up to date before it draws); images come from registered [`renderer::ImageSource`](#renderer-imagesource)s.

Which one to use: [`renderer::TextRenderer`](#renderer-textrenderer) draws plain text overlays from a layout and owns its own atlas pages (the demos' overlay); this draws everything a UI shows, text included, from one list. A program can have both.

It registers with a [`renderer::DeviceResources`](#renderer-deviceresources) at the materials tier; after a reset `Recreate` makes the pipeline and buffers again. The draw list is the caller's and is rebuilt every frame anyway.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | borrows the glyph page owner, the draw list given to `AddToGraph` (until the graph is executed) and the registered image sources; owns its GPU objects. The device and the registry must outlive it. |

### `AddToGraph(RenderGraph*,const ui::DrawList*,GraphResource,rhi::Format,u32,u32,Span<const GraphResource>)` {#renderer-uirenderer-addtograph-rendergraph-star-const-ui-drawlist-star-graphresource-rhi-format-u32-u32-span-lt-const-graphresource-gt}

`method renderer::UiRenderer::AddToGraph` — defined in `engine/renderer/ui_renderer.h`

```cpp
void AddToGraph(RenderGraph* graph, const ui::DrawList* list, GraphResource target, rhi::Format format, u32 width, u32 height, Span<const GraphResource> sampled = Span<const GraphResource>());
```

Declares the UI pass in a frame's graph, after the passes that wrote `target`.

Declares nothing (no pass, no barrier, no cost) when the list has no command. One pass per frame per renderer: the pass reads the list, target and size of the *last* call before the graph runs, and the per-frame buffers hold one list, so two calls in a frame would draw the second list twice; use one renderer per surface or merge the lists. A format different from the current one rebuilds the pipeline here, on the frame's thread.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `graph` | the frame's graph. |
| Parameter `list` | the sorted draw list; borrowed until the graph is executed. |
| Parameter `target` | the display target, already written by an earlier pass; accessed as a colour attachment, read and written. |
| Parameter `format` | the target's format. |
| Parameter `width` | the target's width in pixels. |
| Parameter `height` | the target's height in pixels. |
| Parameter `sampled` | graph resources that earlier passes of this frame write and the list's images sample (a viewport's target imported into the graph); the pass reads them as sampled textures, so the graph orders it after their writers and leaves them in the right state. Default: none (images that live outside the graph). |
| Fails | the pass logs and draws nothing if the pipeline for `format` cannot be made. |
| Aborts | `graph` or `list` is null, `format` is undefined, a size is 0, or the object holds no GPU objects. |
| Ownership | the pass keeps pointers to this object and to `list` until the graph is executed. |


The colour format. An sRGB format makes the UI show the colours the theme says when a material samples it as sRGB (the sampler decodes, the UI pass encodes); a UNORM format stores the colours as they are, for a material that treats them as already linear. It must match the format of the [`renderer::UiRenderer`](#renderer-uirenderer) that draws into it.


The colour the target is cleared to before the UI is drawn, packed `0xAABBGGRR`. Alpha 0 gives a transparent surface (an overlay whose material blends it over the world).

## `UiSurfaceTarget` {#renderer-uisurfacetarget}

`class renderer::UiSurfaceTarget` — defined in `engine/renderer/ui_surface_target.h`

```cpp
class UiSurfaceTarget final : public ImageSource { ... }
```

The offscreen colour texture of one UI surface, drawn by one [`renderer::UiRenderer`](#renderer-uirenderer) and sampled by the world's passes.

It is an [`renderer::ImageSource`](#renderer-imagesource) and a [`renderer::Recreatable`](#renderer-recreatable) (through its [`renderer::ViewportTarget`](#renderer-viewporttarget)), so a material or the UI can sample it and a device reset rebuilds it. After a reset or a resize the texture is empty: `NeedsPaint` is true until the next `AddToGraph`, and the application draws the surface again whether or not it changed. Between paints the texture keeps its last picture, so an idle surface adds no pass and no GPU work.

Per frame: `BeginFrame`, then `AddToGraph` when the surface was rebuilt (or `NeedsPaint`), then `Sampled` wherever a world pass reads it; the returned resource is the same one in both cases, and the graph orders the readers after the paint. Use one `UiRenderer` per surface (its pass is one per frame per renderer).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its textures; the device and the registry must outlive it. |

### `NeedsPaint()` {#renderer-uisurfacetarget-needspaint-const}

`method renderer::UiSurfaceTarget::NeedsPaint` — defined in `engine/renderer/ui_surface_target.h`

```cpp
bool NeedsPaint() const;
```

Returns whether the texture holds no picture of the surface yet (new, resized, recreated after a reset, or the background changed): paint it with `AddToGraph`.

### `AddToGraph(RenderGraph*,UiRenderer*,const ui::DrawList*)` {#renderer-uisurfacetarget-addtograph-rendergraph-star-uirenderer-star-const-ui-drawlist-star}

`method renderer::UiSurfaceTarget::AddToGraph` — defined in `engine/renderer/ui_surface_target.h`

```cpp
GraphResource AddToGraph(RenderGraph* graph, UiRenderer* ui_renderer, const ui::DrawList* list);
```

Paints the surface: imports the texture, clears it to the background and draws `list` over it with `ui_renderer`.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `graph` | the frame's graph. |
| Parameter `ui_renderer` | the renderer that draws this surface; its colour format must be this target's. |
| Parameter `list` | the surface's sorted draw list; borrowed until the graph is executed. An empty list gives a cleared texture. |
| Returns | the graph resource of the texture, written by the paint. |
| Aborts | `graph` or `ui_renderer` is null, the target holds no texture, or the target is already in this frame's graph (paint before `Sampled`, once). |
| Ownership | borrows `graph`, `ui_renderer` and `list`; the resource is valid until the graph is executed. |

Returns the graph resource of the texture for a pass that reads it this frame: the one `AddToGraph` made, or an import of the texture as it was left (no paint this frame). A pass that samples it declares `Read(resource, GraphUsage::Sampled)`.

|---|---|
| Aborts | `graph` is null or the target holds no texture. |
| Ownership | borrows `graph`; the resource is valid until the graph is executed. |

## `ViewportTarget` {#renderer-viewporttarget}

`class renderer::ViewportTarget` — defined in `engine/renderer/viewport_target.h`

```cpp
class ViewportTarget final : public Recreatable, public ImageSource { ... }
```

The render target of one viewport: a colour texture (and optionally a depth texture) of the viewport's size in physical pixels.

A *render target* is a role a texture plays, not a kind of resource: this object owns the textures, and a frame uses the colour one as a `ColorAttachment` while the world is drawn and as a `Sampled` texture while the UI pass reads it. [`renderer::ViewportTarget::ImportColor`](#renderer-viewporttarget-importcolor-rendergraph-star) brings the texture into a frame's graph so the world's passes write it and [`renderer::UiRenderer::AddToGraph`](#renderer-uirenderer-addtograph-rendergraph-star-const-ui-drawlist-star-graphresource-rhi-format-u32-u32-span-lt-const-graphresource-gt) (given the same resource in `sampled`) reads it, in that order.

`Resize` makes the textures of the new size first and destroys the old ones after; the device keeps a destroyed texture alive until the GPU has finished with it, so a frame in flight that still reads the old picture is safe. It is also an [`renderer::ImageSource`](#renderer-imagesource): register it with `UiRenderer::SetImage` and the UI draws it.

It registers with a [`renderer::DeviceResources`](#renderer-deviceresources) at the textures tier. After a reset or a loss `Recreate` makes textures of the same size again; their contents are lost until the next frame draws the world (the UI shows nothing for an image whose texture is missing, and the picture returns one frame after the reset).

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Thread-safety | owner-thread-only. |
| Ownership | owns its textures; the device and the registry must outlive it. |

`method renderer::ViewportTarget::Create` — defined in `engine/renderer/viewport_target.h`


| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Parameter `device` | the device. |
| Parameter `desc` | the formats. |
| Parameter `width` | width in pixels; at least 1. |
| Parameter `height` | height in pixels; at least 1. |
| Parameter `resources` | the registry, or null for a target that does not need to survive a reset. |
| Parameter `debug_name` | a name for debuggers; copied. |
| Returns | true on success. |

| Ownership | the device and the registry are borrowed. |

### `Resize(u32,u32)` {#renderer-viewporttarget-resize-u32-u32}

`method renderer::ViewportTarget::Resize` — defined in `engine/renderer/viewport_target.h`

```cpp
bool Resize(u32 width, u32 height);
```

Gives the target a new size: makes the new textures, then destroys the old ones. The same size again does nothing and returns true. Between a `Release` and the `Recreate` after it only the size is recorded, and the recreate uses it.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | true when the target has the requested size afterwards. |
| Fails | returns false, keeping the old textures and size, when a size is 0, above the device limit, or the new textures cannot be made. |
| Aborts | the object does not exist. |

### `ImportColor(RenderGraph*)` {#renderer-viewporttarget-importcolor-rendergraph-star}

`method renderer::ViewportTarget::ImportColor` — defined in `engine/renderer/viewport_target.h`

```cpp
GraphResource ImportColor(RenderGraph* graph);
```

Brings the colour texture into `graph` for this frame. The graph leaves it in the sampled state, which is also where the next frame finds it. The target records that new state at import, so the graph must be executed: a frame abandoned after the import (the build callback returns false) leaves the record wrong; call `Resize` to the same size plus one pixel and back, or recreate the target, to reset it.

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Returns | the graph resource the world's last pass writes (as `ColorAttachment`) and the UI pass reads (in `sampled`). |
| Aborts | `graph` is null or the object holds no texture. |
| Ownership | borrows `graph`; the resource is valid until the graph is executed. |
